	<item>
		<title>Introducing the Amazon Bedrock Trusted Access API, with Enterprise Frontier Safeguards and Enterprise Data Controls for Claude models</title>
		<link>https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/introducing-the-amazon-bedrock-trusted-access-api-with-anthropics-enterprise-frontier-safeguards-and-enterprise-data-controls/</link>
		
		<dc:creator><![CDATA[Dani Mitchell]]></dc:creator>
		<pubDate>Tue, 24 Nov 2026 05:01:14 +0000</pubDate>
				<category><![CDATA[Amazon Bedrock]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Announcements]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Intermediate (200)]]></category>
		<guid isPermaLink="false">76b2285b501c3518d9f413fc227aa9b97a5e6b72</guid>

					<description>Amazon Bedrock now offers a trusted access program API: one place to discover, request, and enroll in model provider programs. The first two offerings, in public preview, are Anthropic's Enterprise Frontier Safeguards (EFS) and Enterprise Data Controls (EDC), which give you control over your data when you use Claude Fable models.</description>
										<content:encoded>&lt;p&gt;Today we are announcing the general availability of the trusted access program API on &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock&lt;/a&gt;. The API gives you one place to discover, request, and enroll in model provider trusted access programs. We are also announcing the public preview of the first two offerings for Claude models: &lt;a href="https://claude.com/resources/articles/zero-data-retention-with-enterprise-frontier-safeguards"&gt;Enterprise Frontier Safeguards&lt;/a&gt; (EFS) and Enterprise Data Controls (EDC). Both are available on Amazon Bedrock, including Claude Fable, Claude Mythos, and Claude Opus 5.5 and Sonnet 5.5 used through the &lt;a href="https://support.claude.com/en/articles/14604842-cyber-verification-program" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Cyber Verification Program&lt;/a&gt; (CVP).&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Frontier AI labs require stricter safety monitoring policies to their most capable models, and those policies still apply on Amazon Bedrock. For eligible customers, model providers also offer customers more control on how those policies are met, such as where retained data is stored and how it is reviewed. Each offering has its own terms, approvals, and settings.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The Amazon Bedrock trusted access program API gives every provider offering one consistent Amazon Bedrock experience. Your teams discover offerings, check eligibility, and enroll in the same way for every provider. Your administrators decide who can enroll, using existing IAM policies and service control policies. As more model providers add offerings, those appear in the same place.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;EFS and EDC were built in partnership between AWS and Anthropic. They let you access Anthropic’s most capable models safely, from both a model use and a customer data perspective. With both EFS and EDC, when automated safety monitoring finds a risk, it creates a Safety Case for your team to review. With EFS, customers store their data in their own AWS account, and prompts and completions are not retained by Anthropic or Amazon Bedrock. With EDC, retained data is stored in Amazon-managed storage instead of your account and prompts and completions are not retained by Anthropic. One application gives access to both offerings.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;In this post, we explain how the trusted access program API works, then walk through EFS and EDC storage options and setup.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="how-trusted-access-programs-work"&gt;How trusted access programs work&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;A trusted access program is a named provider program that gives customers more control of safety reviews and access to model capabilities in exchange for accepting the program’s terms. Amazon Bedrock trusted access program API now gives you one place to find the model and use case programs available to your account. EFS and EDC are the first offerings available today. Offerings that providers add later appear in the same place and follow the same steps.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Anyone with access to the trusted access program API can list the offerings and check their status for an account, an organization in AWS Organizations, or an Amazon Bedrock project. The status tells you what to do next. &lt;code&gt;eligible&lt;/code&gt; means you’re eligible for the offering and can enroll, &lt;code&gt;requestable&lt;/code&gt; means you need approval for eligibility first, and the &lt;code&gt;statusReason&lt;/code&gt; field in the response explains how to request it. The model provider (or AWS, as authorized by the model provider) approves your eligibility.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Enrolling is a separate step where you accept the offering’s terms imposed by the provider. You enroll at the scope where you were approved: an account, every account in an organization from the management account, or a single project. You can opt out at any time. Each step is a call to the trusted access program API, so you control who can enroll with AWS Identity and Access Management (IAM) policies and service control policies (SCPs). More information is available in the &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/trusted-access-programs.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;trusted access programs documentation&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="enterprise-frontier-safeguards"&gt;Enterprise Frontier Safeguards&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;With Enterprise Frontier Safeguards (EFS), you can retain your data in your own AWS environment with full control. It is designed with over 100 customers, including a quarter of the Fortune 100 and many companies in industries with strict rules on data visibility, like healthcare, financial services, and law.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Automated safety monitoring looks for patterns that only show up across many requests. These include intentional misuse that could lead to serious harm, such as offensive cyber operations. They also include agents that go beyond what a user asked for, such as using a software exploit to reach an internal system.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;On Amazon Bedrock, AWS operates EFS and the customer inputs and outputs stay within the customer’s AWS account. Only automated safety monitoring within AWS reads the retained inputs and outputs. AWS human reviewers don’t review content for EFS. Your review stays with you. Neither AWS nor Anthropic humans access your retained inputs or outputs. Your inputs and outputs are never used for training.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="how-it-works"&gt;How it works&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Amazon Bedrock reaches your &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/kms/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Key Management Service&lt;/a&gt; (AWS KMS) key and &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/s3/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Simple Storage Service&lt;/a&gt; (Amazon S3) bucket as a single AWS service principal and only through the key policy and bucket policy that you write. You can remove that access at any time, but Amazon Bedrock stops serving the covered models 24 hours after it loses access to your key or bucket.&lt;img class="wp-image-146150 aligncenter" src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/24/ecf6fcd5b73dc5eb-efs-figure1-v10b.png" alt="" width="800" height="476"&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;When you call a covered model including Claude Fable, Claude Mythos, and Claude Opus 5.5 and Sonnet 5.5 used through the Cyber Verification Program (CVP):&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ol type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Your application calls the model. Inference works as it does for any Amazon Bedrock model.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Amazon Bedrock writes the prompt and response to its retention and key services.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;These services encrypt the data with your AWS KMS key.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Amazon Bedrock writes the prompt and response (together, “Sealed Safety Data”) to your bucket, encrypted with your key.&amp;nbsp;When writing to your bucket, Amazon writes the content within minutes. On some failures with your key or bucket, we can retry for up to 72 hours.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Automated safety monitoring, which runs in AWS owned and operated accounts, scans the data and looks for misuse across requests.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;If automated safety monitoring finds a potential risk, a case is created in your bucket for your review. For details, &lt;a href="https://trust.anthropic.com/resources?s=a7lwkeieqk3ggz29k954j&amp;amp;name=enterprise-frontier-safeguards:-technical-overview"&gt;see&amp;nbsp;Enterprise Frontier Safeguards: Technical Overview&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="how-your-team-handles-cases"&gt;How your team handles cases&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;A Case summarizes what automated safety monitoring found and references the related transcripts. Retained records arrive under the &lt;code&gt;transcripts/&lt;/code&gt; prefix in your bucket, and case files arrive under &lt;code&gt;review-cases/&lt;/code&gt;. Your team reviews each Case and writes a response file to &lt;code&gt;review-responses/&lt;/code&gt;, stating whether it found a violation (&lt;code&gt;violation&lt;/code&gt; or &lt;code&gt;no_violation&lt;/code&gt;) and, for a violation, the applicable policy category.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="enterprise-data-controls"&gt;Enterprise Data Controls&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;EDC offers the same automated safety monitoring as EFS and human review of flagged activity stays with your team. Unlike EFS, EDC stores retained data in Amazon managed storage, encrypted with AWS managed keys, so there’s no bucket or key to set up. Eligible customers can access both EFS and EDC through a single enrollment application. If your policy allows retained data to stay in Amazon-managed storage and encrypted with AWS-managed keys, enroll in EDC. If your policy instead requires retained data to be encrypted with your AWS KMS key and stay in your S3 bucket, enroll in EFS. Either way, your team will be able to review flagged activity.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Outside EFS and EDC, you can use Claude Fable models on Amazon Bedrock in two ways today:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;With the &lt;code&gt;aws_review&lt;/code&gt; data retention mode, Amazon Bedrock retains inputs and outputs for up to 30 days, where they are available for review within the AWS boundary.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Eligible customers can use current Fable models for internal use with zero data retention (ZDR) through at least June 30, 2027. EFS is the long-term path for ensuring neither AWS nor Anthropic retains your prompts and completions after the interim ZDR exception ends.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="choose-where-retained-data-is-stored"&gt;Choose where retained data is stored&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;With EFS, retained data is stored only in your own S3 bucket, encrypted with your KMS key. Neither Anthropic nor AWS keeps it in their own storage. The following table compares EFS with the other routes.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;table border="1px" width="100%" cellpadding="10px"&gt; 
 &lt;tbody&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Not Eligible for ZDR, EFS, or EDC&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;ZDR exception (interim)&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;EFS, your own Amazon S3 bucket (public preview)&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;EDC, Amazon-managed storage (public preview)&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Data retention&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Up to 30 days&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;None, through at least June 30, 2027, for current Fable models and existing Cyber Verification Program models for internal use&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Amazon Bedrock keeps no copy. Your lifecycle rules apply, 30 days minimum.&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Up to 30 days, then Amazon Bedrock deletes it&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Where the retained data is stored&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;AWS managed storage&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Not retained&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Your Amazon S3 bucket&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Amazon-managed storage&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Encryption key&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;AWS managed&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Not applicable&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Your AWS Key Management Service (AWS KMS) key&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;AWS managed&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Safety monitoring&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Available for review within the AWS boundary&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;None&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Automated. Cases arrive in your bucket for your team to review.&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Automated. Your team reviews flagged Cases. Cases aren’t delivered during the public preview.&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;What your &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/cloudtrail/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS CloudTrail&lt;/a&gt; shows&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Your Amazon Bedrock API calls&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Your Amazon Bedrock API calls&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Your Amazon Bedrock API calls, plus Amazon Bedrock’s use of your key and bucket&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Your Amazon Bedrock API calls&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Storage cost&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Not applicable&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Not applicable&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Usual Amazon S3 and AWS KMS charges&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Not applicable&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
 &lt;/tbody&gt; 
&lt;/table&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;em&gt;Table 1: Routes for Claude Fable models on Amazon Bedrock.&lt;/em&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Storage configurations apply to one Region at a time, and a Region without a configuration uses Amazon-managed storage. Save a configuration in every Region where you call covered models.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="getting-started"&gt;Getting started&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Start by checking your EFS status with the trusted access program API:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-bash"&gt;aws bedrock list-trusted-access-programs --region us-east-1&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;If the status is &lt;code&gt;eligible&lt;/code&gt;, your account is approved. If it’s &lt;code&gt;requestable&lt;/code&gt;, follow the steps in the &lt;code&gt;statusReason&lt;/code&gt; field to request access. Joining the preview turns off the interim ZDR exception for the accounts you add.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;After you’re approved, set up EFS in each Region where you call covered models:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ol type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Create an AWS KMS key and an Amazon S3 bucket&lt;/strong&gt; in the same Region. The key and bucket policies give Amazon Bedrock access only through its service principal and only for your account’s data.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Give your role permission&lt;/strong&gt; to save the storage configuration.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Save your storage configuration.&lt;/strong&gt; Amazon Bedrock checks your key and bucket settings before it saves, and the error names any check that fails.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Set your data retention mode&lt;/strong&gt; to &lt;code&gt;aws_review&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Call a Claude Fable model&lt;/strong&gt;, then confirm that AWS CloudTrail shows Amazon Bedrock using your key. Retained records usually appear in your bucket within minutes but can take up to 72 hours if Amazon Bedrock has to retry.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/anthropic-efs-enterprise-data-controls.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;EFS documentation&lt;/a&gt; has the complete key policy, bucket policy, IAM policy, and API calls for each step. It also covers organization and project configurations, and troubleshooting. To use enterprise data controls instead, skip steps 1 and 2 and choose Amazon-managed storage in step 3.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="conclusion"&gt;Conclusion&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The trusted access program API, now generally available, gives you one way to find model provider offerings on Amazon Bedrock, check your status, and enroll, with IAM policies and SCPs controlling who can opt in. EFS and EDC are the first offerings in public preview. They give you control over your data when you use Claude Fable and Cyber Verification Program models. With your own Amazon S3 bucket, Amazon Bedrock keeps no copy of the retained data, your AWS KMS key encrypts it, and every access appears in your own AWS CloudTrail logs.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;To get started, check your EFS status with &lt;code&gt;aws bedrock list-trusted-access-programs&lt;/code&gt; and enroll in the EFS preview. To learn more, see the &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/trusted-access-programs.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;trusted access programs documentation&lt;/a&gt; and the &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/anthropic-efs-enterprise-data-controls.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;EFS/EDC documentation&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;To enroll in the EFS and EDC public preview, work with your AWS account team to submit a request. Have the AWS account IDs ready (or Amazon Bedrock runtime project ARNs for projects) and note that the preview is for customers already enrolled in EFS ZDR.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;hr style="width: 100%"&gt;

		<item>
		<title>Meta ads is now available in AWS Quick</title>
		<link>https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/meta-ads-is-now-available-in-aws-quick/</link>
		
		<dc:creator><![CDATA[Chris Geiger]]></dc:creator>
		<pubDate>Sun, 22 Nov 2026 01:59:13 +0000</pubDate>
				<category><![CDATA[Announcements]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[AWS Quick]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Foundational (100)]]></category>
		<guid isPermaLink="false">2f499b0fad46f2d46fc1c337c454cd6adc7edd47</guid>

					<description>AWS Quick now has a Meta ads AI connector. Meta advertisers can use natural language in Quick to create, manage, and analyze campaigns while staying connected to the Meta ad management network, and combine Meta ad data with first- and third-party sources to build dashboards, documents, slide decks, and automated workflows.</description>
										<content:encoded>&lt;p&gt;Marketers have never had more data to act on. Take an apparel brand that advertises on Meta technologies: campaign results live in Meta Ads Manager, briefs and creative assets in shared storage, and inventory, customer lifetime value, and in-store purchases in a data lake. Analysts export reports, merge spreadsheets, and rebuild the same deck each week, and the data ages before anyone acts on it. When the team does decide what to change, someone returns to Ads Manager to update campaigns, ad sets, budgets, and audiences by hand. As a result, teams judge campaigns on metrics alone, and the path from insight to action stays slow and manual.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Today, AWS Quick added a Meta ads AI connector. The connector gives Quick access to ads Model Context Protocol (MCP) server operations. Meta ads customers can use natural language to create, manage, and analyze campaigns within Quick, while staying connected to the Meta ad management network. Marketers can complete tasks with support from AI, such as pulling insights to understand ads performance, creating product catalogs, and editing existing or creating new campaigns. Quick can also combine Meta ad management data with first- and third-party data sources to create dashboards, documents, slide decks, and automated workflows.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;blockquote&gt;
 &lt;p&gt;&lt;em&gt;“Meta ads AI connector through Quick gives our marketing teams one place to go from campaign planning to execution. It lets us act on business insights directly in our ads, without the handoffs and tool switching that slow campaigns down. With fal.ai’s image, audio, and video models in the same workflow, we’ll test more creative ideas and spend more time reaching customers.”&lt;/em&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
 &lt;p&gt;— Tina Sang, Head of Marketing at fal.ai&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/blockquote&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="demo-meta-ads-in-quick"&gt;Demo: Meta ads in Quick&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The video shows how to install the Meta ads connector from &lt;strong&gt;Customize&lt;/strong&gt; &amp;gt; &lt;strong&gt;Connectors&lt;/strong&gt; in AWS Quick, sign in with Facebook, and choose which Pages, Businesses, and Instagram accounts AWS Quick can access. It then runs two example prompts. One turns past campaign results into an eight-slide launch-plan deck. The other builds a dashboard comparing spend, leads, and cost per lead for two ad sets, with recommended tests to run next.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 640px;" class="wp-video"&gt;
 &lt;video class="wp-video-shortcode" id="video-145451-1" width="640" height="360" preload="metadata" controls="controls"&gt;
  &lt;source type="video/mp4" src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/artifacts/DBSBlogs/ML-22411/b46d9e222b1ec27f572a3e2ba0fc06ab8c1447b3be0e8fd10a7e101091d9191d.mp4?_=1"&gt;
 &lt;/video&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;em&gt;Connecting Meta ads to Quick and running prompts for performance analysis, campaign creation, and a shareable deck&lt;/em&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="example-use-cases-and-prompts"&gt;Example use cases and prompts&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The following use cases demonstrate how Meta advertisers can use the connector with Quick, along with sample prompts.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;table border="1px" width="100%" cellpadding="10px"&gt; 
 &lt;tbody&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Use case&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;What Quick does&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Example prompt&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Plan and launch campaigns&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Drafts campaign, ad set, and ad settings from an approved brief. Flags missing budget and audience decisions. Runs a standard pre-launch check of naming, UTM tags, budget, and special ad category.&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;“Using Meta ads, [campaign_brief], and past results, propose a launch plan for [promotion_name]. Flag missing decisions and prepare a review deck. Do not create or change ads yet.”&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Analyze and share performance&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Tracks spend, CTR, and ROAS at the account, campaign, ad set, and ad level. Compares results to the media plan. Builds a weekly deck of top ads, weak ads, and next tests. Lets leaders review the deck and ask follow-up questions before meetings.&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;“Analyze Meta ads conversions for [campaign_name], identify the biggest changes, and recommend next tests. Put the findings in a PowerPoint I can share with my team. Do not change budgets.”&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Refresh creative and audiences&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Flags creative fatigue when frequency rises and CTR falls. Creates new Custom Audiences after approval. Compares the best-performing themes with competitor messaging and trends. Drafts the next creative brief with headline variants and an A/B test plan.&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;“Use Meta ads results, [past_briefs], and approved audience information to flag possible creative fatigue and propose new messaging and segments. Draft a creative brief and test hypotheses, without activating changes.”&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Measure lift and customer quality&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Helps plan a conversion lift study. Confirms eligibility and creates the study after approval. Compares lift with attributed conversions and customer value from the CRM. Presents the results, the level of uncertainty, and the budget implications.&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;“Help me plan a conversion lift study in Meta ads for [campaign_name]. Check eligibility, prepare the inputs for approval, and explain how study results and [customer_outcomes] could inform our next decisions.”&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Prepare product catalogs for catalog ads&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Builds a SKU sheet that joins catalog eligibility with stock, margin, and image status. Explains why Meta rejected items and updates product sets. Gives merchandisers one place to review rejected items. Routes items to data owners and tracks fixes.&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;“Use Meta ads catalog diagnostics and [product_dataset] to identify ineligible products and image issues. Build a SKU correction table and propose products for catalog ads using authorized stock and margin inputs.”&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Align spend with stock and profit&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Shows Meta spend next to warehouse stock and contribution margin for each product line. Models how shifting budget to high-margin, in-stock products would affect results. Runs scheduled stock checks and queues changes for approval. Applies the changes in Meta ads after approval.&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;“Compare Meta ads performance with [warehouse_inventory], [margin_dataset], and [media_plan]. Propose where to shift spend and when a lower ROAS target could meet our approved commercial goals. Show assumptions and do not apply changes.”&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Diagnose pixel, account, and delivery issues&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Triages pixel and Conversions API issues, such as drops in event volume and low Event Match Quality scores. Passes an Ads Manager error page into a troubleshooting flow. Gathers diagnostics, internal order counts, and Meta help articles into one checklist. Drafts a handoff email to engineering.&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;“Investigate [issue] in Meta ads for [ad_account]. Compare available diagnostics with [order_dataset], find relevant guidance, and separate confirmed errors from hypotheses. Draft a troubleshooting handoff without changing the account.”&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Govern spend and connect it to your data&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Routes budget increases above a set threshold to finance for approval. Tracks spend against approved budgets for each ad account. Reconciles spend exports with warehouse and finance records. Keeps budget policies, metric definitions, and spend reviews in one shared place.&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;“Review Meta ads spend against [budget_policy], reconcile reporting with [warehouse_dataset], and use [change_log] to document known changes. Produce an exception report and proposed review steps, without executing changes.”&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
 &lt;/tbody&gt; 
&lt;/table&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Note:&lt;/strong&gt; The connector can make changes in your ad accounts. Before Quick creates a campaign, changes a budget, or runs another write action, it asks for your permission to take the action.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="prerequisites"&gt;Prerequisites&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Before you begin, confirm that you have the following:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Access to Quick.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;A Meta business account with access to the ad accounts you want to connect.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="get-started"&gt;Get started&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href="https://quick.aws.com/sn?offer_type=meta-ads-quick-promo" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Try Quick at no additional cost for 3 months&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Sign up, connect your Meta ads account, and ask your first question.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Invite up to 10 teammates at no cost for your first 3 months.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt;

		<item>
		<title>New in AWS Quick: More capable flows, an updated web experience, and agents that work together</title>
		<link>https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/new-in-aws-quick-more-capable-flows-an-updated-web-experience-and-agents-that-work-together/</link>
		
		<dc:creator><![CDATA[Hillary Lovric]]></dc:creator>
		<pubDate>Sat, 21 Nov 2026 01:06:42 +0000</pubDate>
				<category><![CDATA[Announcements]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[AWS Quick]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Foundational (100)]]></category>
		<guid isPermaLink="false">02c52259bc83b8aa57b8410eb8c813a22ed5fd28</guid>

					<description>AWS Quick is the AI workspace built only for work. Today we're releasing updates that help your team get more done: build and run business-process flows in chat, triage your day in the updated web app, get more complete answers from multiple agents at once, and connect new data sources like Meta ads and Amplitude.</description>
										<content:encoded>&lt;p&gt;Turn one person’s process into your team’s process, get more done in the Quick web app, and get more complete answers from agents working together.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;AWS Quick is the AI workspace built only for work. Today, we are releasing updates that help your team get more of its everyday work done:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Build, see, and improve business processes, all through conversation.&lt;/strong&gt; Describe a process in chat and Quick builds a flow to orchestrate agents, skills, and tools with access to structured data, knowledge, and apps. New deterministic steps help make your workflows consistent and verifiable, now with longer-running workflows and support for large data volumes.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Triage your day and keep agents working in the Quick web app.&lt;/strong&gt; The updated web app puts what needs your attention and a daily briefing on your home screen, adds the agents and skills you use on desktop, and keeps your work running after you close the tab.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Complete work spanning multiple agents through a single prompt.&lt;/strong&gt; With multi-agent orchestration, several specialized agents work on your request at the same time, and Quick combines what they find into one response.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Bring more of your data into Quick.&lt;/strong&gt; Connect to Meta ads and Amplitude, then ask in natural language and combine the answers with your other sources.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Skills on web, security and governance improvements, and more languages:&lt;/strong&gt; Turn the way you do a task into a skill Quick can reuse, now in the Quick web app. Set configurable content safety and sensitive-information controls for user prompts. Support for Japanese, German, Chinese (Simplified and Traditional), and Vietnamese.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;AI assistants make individuals faster, but the gains often stay with one person. The way one analyst builds the monthly report rarely becomes the way the team builds it, and work waits until someone is back at their desk. This release helps your team turn what one person knows into a process everyone can rely on, and keeps work moving when you step away.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="turn-one-persons-process-into-your-teams-process"&gt;Turn one person’s process into your team’s process&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;AWS Quick Flows already helps teams automate and standardize business processes so people can focus on their most important work. Now you can build, run, and refine flows entirely in chat. And your flows can take on more of your tough work, whether you start it from the web, desktop, or mobile app.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;For example, here is how flows could support a finance team. Maya is a financial planning and analysis manager at a retailer. Each month, she prepares a report for her VP of Finance that explains changes to costs and margins. For each region, she pulls last month’s results, compares them to the plan in a spreadsheet, writes commentary on the biggest changes, and sends the report. The process lives in her memory and a personal checklist. When she is out, a teammate has to rebuild the report from scratch, and it looks different every time.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;With Quick, Maya can standardize the process, automate it, and make it available to her team. She describes her process in chat, the way she would explain it to a new employee. Quick builds the flow from her description and she tests and refines it in the same conversation. A diagram beside the chat shows each step, so she can confirm it matches how she works.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div id="attachment_145991" style="width: 1486px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-145991" loading="lazy" class="wp-image-145991 size-full" src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/20/Screenshot-2026-11-20-at-4.58.42%E2%80%AFPM.png" alt="" width="1476" height="1526"&gt;
 &lt;p id="caption-attachment-145991" class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 1: Maya describes her monthly report in chat, and Quick builds the flow with a diagram beside it. Both views, enlarged here for visibility, appear side by side in the same Quick window&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;img loading="lazy" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-145990" src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/20/Screenshot-2026-11-20-at-4.58.51%E2%80%AFPM.png" alt="" width="1302" height="1170"&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;A flow can now bring together agents, skills, tools, and data, and handle longer processes with more data. Fixed calculations and checks keep the results consistent, agents draft and analyze, and Maya decides what goes in the final report. On each run, her flow:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ol type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Pulls last month’s results from the team’s monthly results dashboard in Quick, so she starts from the numbers her team already reports on.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Works through several regions at the same time, so the report is ready sooner, even as the company adds regions.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Compares each region to the annual plan in the team’s budget spreadsheet, using set formulas so the numbers are calculated the same way each month.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Flags regions that moved significantly from plan, based on thresholds Maya set, and drafts commentary on each using the team’s commentary skill.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Drafts a Microsoft Excel summary to the team’s shared Space and report email for Maya to review, and tells the team in Slack that the draft is ready.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Maya publishes the flow for her team. At the next month-end, she is at a planning meeting, so Jordan, an analyst on her team, runs it from chat in the Quick desktop app. There, the flow can use a spreadsheet of regional adjustments saved on his computer. Before the report goes out, Jordan reviews the draft, including why each region was flagged, asks for a few changes in chat, and approves it for the VP of Finance. When Priya joins the team, she runs the same flow at her first month-end, and the report is built the same way.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;While this is a fictional example, real customers tell us they’re automating complex business processes to make them more consistent and repeatable. This gives their people back valuable time to focus on more strategic work. Take Pomelo, a Latin American fintech provider powering card issuance, processing, and payment operations for customers:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;blockquote&gt;
 &lt;p&gt;&lt;em&gt;“At Pomelo, AWS Quick changed who gets to build solutions. Before, every analysis or automation depended on the data team. Today, people across every area explore information, make decisions, and build their own agents and flows within a unified, governed environment. Without growing our team, nearly four times as many people now work with data each month, so our data team can focus on its most complex, high-impact work. A single use case already saves us 80 hours of work per month. But the real transformation isn’t the hours we save. Quick is transforming our data and innovation culture: we’ve gone from a few people building solutions for many to an organization where everyone can create, experiment, and change the way they work. And we’re just getting started.”&lt;/em&gt;&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/blockquote&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;— Rodrigo Marini, Head of Data, Pomelo Fintech Services S.A.S.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="get-more-done-in-the-updated-quick-web-app"&gt;Get more done in the updated Quick web app&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;If you already use Quick in your browser, the updated web app puts the items that need your attention on the home screen as soon as you sign in. A reorganized left navigation brings agents, flows, skills, knowledge, and connectors together under Customize. It includes the agents and skills you built on desktop and ones others have shared with you. Your existing dashboards, topics, and spaces work as before, and you do not need to turn anything on.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;See what needs your attention, then act on it.&lt;/strong&gt; Your feed and a new daily briefing bring together the email and messages that need you, in priority order. Ask Quick about any item or have it draft replies for you to review and send.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Keep agents working when you step away.&lt;/strong&gt; Schedule a task or start a longer request before a meeting, and Quick keeps working after you close your browser. When you return, review the results and respond to any agents waiting on you in Mission Control.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Find your work in one place.&lt;/strong&gt; Library brings together your recent work, favorites, pinned items, and the documents and files you create in Quick. Your conversations, agents, and files sync across web, desktop, and mobile, so you can start in your browser and pick up on your phone or in the desktop app, where Quick can work with files on your computer.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/20/ML-22322-3.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/20/ML-22322-3.png" alt="AWS Quick web app feed showing email and messages that need a reply in one view" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 2: The feed brings the morning’s email and messages that need a reply into one view&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="get-more-complete-answers-from-agents-working-together"&gt;Get more complete answers from agents working together&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Some requests need the knowledge of more than one specialized agent. With multi-agent orchestration, the agent you ask can bring in several specialized agents at once, each working on the part it knows best. The agent then combines their work into one response. Each agent keeps its own instructions, knowledge, and context, so the people who know that work best can build and maintain it.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;For example, you might ask why shipping costs rose in one region. Your agent can bring in a forecasting agent built on your team’s planning data and a procurement agent built on the procurement team’s vendor contracts. You get one response that shows what each agent contributed, and you decide what to do with it.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="connect-quick-to-more-of-the-tools-your-team-already-uses"&gt;Connect Quick to more of the tools your team already uses&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Work lives in many systems. This release adds new connectors so Quick can pull from the analytics and tools your team uses every day. Connect once, then ask in natural language.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Meta ads.&lt;/strong&gt; Create, manage, and analyze Meta ad campaigns in natural language through a connector to Meta’s ads Model Context Protocol (MCP) server. Quick combines Meta ad data with your first-party and third-party sources, and asks for your permission before it creates a campaign, changes a budget, or takes any other write action in an ad account. &lt;a href="https://quick.aws.com/sn?offer_type=meta-ads-quick-promo" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Try Quick free for 3 months&lt;/a&gt; and invite up to 10 teammates at no cost.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Amplitude.&lt;/strong&gt; Ask about product usage, customer behavior, and conversion in natural language. Quick connects to Amplitude through its MCP server, so product and marketing teams can pull behavioral insights into the dashboards, documents, and workflows they build in Quick.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Connectors respect your existing permissions. Quick only shows each person what they already have access to. Quick asks before it takes any write action. Administrators choose which connectors teams can add.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="also-in-this-release"&gt;Also in this release&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Skills in the Quick web app.&lt;/strong&gt; Skills are reusable instructions that teach Quick to do a task your way, such as reviewing a contract against your team’s checklist, or writing a project update in your company’s format. You can now browse, install, create, and manage skills in the web app, as on desktop.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Guardrails for prompts (preview).&lt;/strong&gt; Administrators can set policies that filter content, block denied topics and words, detect prompt attacks, and protect selected types of personal information in the prompts people send to Quick. They can test a policy in detect-only mode before enforcing it, then apply it to individual users, identity provider groups, or the whole account. This feature is available to all AWS Quick customers subscribed to Professional and Enterprise plans.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Permissions and limits by group.&lt;/strong&gt; Administrators can now assign custom permissions and limit profiles to groups from your identity provider, in addition to individual users, roles, and the whole account. For example, you can prevent your finance group from creating agents, or set how many agent hours each member of a group can use, without managing each person separately. This feature is available to all AWS Quick customers subscribed to Professional and Enterprise plans.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Choose which data sources people can add.&lt;/strong&gt; Administrators can now control which types of data sources people can create, update, or share. For example, you can let people connect Amazon Redshift while blocking file uploads, or stop people from sharing data sources from a specific connector. This feature is available to all AWS Quick customers subscribed to Professional and Enterprise plans.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;More languages.&lt;/strong&gt; Use Quick in Japanese, German, Chinese (Simplified and Traditional), and Vietnamese.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="every-decision-stays-yours"&gt;Every decision stays yours&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Whether you build a monthly report, run one your manager built, or set up Quick for your organization, Quick works the way you do. Your data stays in your environment and does not train anyone else’s models. You bring the judgment, and Quick gives your team the capacity to match its goals.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;These features are available today in all Regions where AWS Quick is available.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;To try what’s new, &lt;a href="https://signup.aws.amazon.com/billing/signup/service?app=AmazonQuickSuite&amp;amp;tier=free&amp;amp;funnel=boost&amp;amp;trk=0ea79374-057c-4897-84f0-5fe792905a8f&amp;amp;sc_channel=ps&amp;amp;additionalState=ampDeviceId%3D0690ae49-ceec-4845-a08d-698221f6e326%26ampSessionId%3D1795214231829#/validation" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;sign in to Quick&lt;/a&gt; or &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/quick/download/?trk=0ea79374-057c-4897-84f0-5fe792905a8f&amp;amp;sc_channel=ps" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;download Quick&lt;/a&gt; and get started in minutes. To learn about enterprise pricing plans, talk to your &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/forms/quick-contact-us?trk=ece6a9f3-45c3-4893-a5f0-a5a64813a937&amp;amp;sc_channel=el&amp;amp;refid=0ea79374-057c-4897-84f0-5fe792905a8f" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS account team&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;

  &lt;p style="overflow: hidden"&gt;Hillary is a Senior Product Marketing Manager for AWS Quick. She previously built and led product marketing and marketing organizations at FinTech startups across Canada and the US. She now focuses on go-to-market strategy for AWS Quick, helping customers see how AI can take on real work for them and their teams. Hillary lives in Seattle and enjoys skiing, traveling, and spending time on the ocean.&lt;/p&gt;

			</item>
		<item>
		<title>Cut self-hosted LLM cost and latency with the distributed KV caching agent skill</title>
		<link>https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/cut-self-hosted-llm-cost-and-latency-with-the-distributed-kv-caching-agent-skill/</link>
		
		<dc:creator><![CDATA[Ravi Yadav]]></dc:creator>
		<pubDate>Sat, 21 Nov 2026 00:14:26 +0000</pubDate>
				<category><![CDATA[Amazon ElastiCache]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Expert (400)]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Technical How-to]]></category>
		<guid isPermaLink="false">d7bdb91bc1e6b32644ddf96aa43ed4840d83f174</guid>

					<description>Self-hosting LLMs on GPUs means paying to recompute the same context on every turn. Distributed KV caching with Amazon ElastiCache can cut inference cost by up to 40% and time to first token by up to 80%. This post walks through the distributed KV caching skill in the Agent Toolkit for AWS, which evaluates and sets up the cache from your coding agent.</description>
										<content:encoded>&lt;p&gt;Customers increasingly self-host large language models (LLMs) on GPUs for control over cost, accuracy, and latency. However, self-hosting often means paying for redundant GPU work because small, isolated caches on each server can’t hold or share the growing context AI agents and chat applications resend. Consider a legal AI assistant that can accumulate hundreds of thousands of tokens of case history in each conversation. When a lawyer returns to a thread after lunch, responses from the LLM can take minutes. The GPU server that picks up the request has none of that history in its memory. So, the GPU recomputes the key-value (KV) attention states for all of those tokens, a step called prefill, before it writes a word. The same pattern slows down resumed coding agents, agentic AI assistants, and document Q&amp;amp;A assistants. Distributed KV caching with Amazon ElastiCache can cut self-hosted LLM inference costs by up to 40% and time to first token (TTFT) by up to 80%. Distributed KV caching extends GPU-local caches with a shared tier beside your GPU fleet so any server can reuse KV state for days across sessions, routing shifts, and server failures. “&lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/database/announcing-distributed-kv-caching-for-self-hosted-llms-with-amazon-elasticache/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Announcing distributed KV caching for self-hosted LLMs with Amazon ElastiCache&lt;/a&gt;” dives into the technical details and the performance benchmarks.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;This post details the &lt;a href="https://github.com/aws/agent-toolkit-for-aws/tree/main/skills/specialized-skills/database-skills/distributed-kv-caching" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;distributed KV caching skill&lt;/a&gt; in the Agent Toolkit for AWS. With this skill, you can evaluate and set up distributed KV caching from within your coding agent in a few prompts. The skill guides the agent through checking whether your workload benefits from distributed KV caching, setting up a cache cluster for testing or production workload, and troubleshooting your LLM inference caching. The skill works with &lt;a href="https://kiro.dev/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Kiro&lt;/a&gt;, Claude Code, OpenAI Codex, and other coding agents that support Agent Skills. We walk through how the skill works with four conversations: diagnosing a slow first token, sizing and pricing the cache, setting it up, and fixing a failed transfer.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="why-a-skill-for-kv-caching-and-what-can-it-do"&gt;Why a skill for KV caching and what can it do?&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The benefits for your workload depend on characteristics such as LLM models used, user traffic patterns, and prompt routing choices, and can be complex to estimate. We created the &lt;a href="https://github.com/aws-samples/amazon-elasticache-samples/tree/main/kvtrace" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;KVTrace tool&lt;/a&gt; to get close estimates of the benefits for your workload, from your own model and traffic before you deploy any infrastructure, at no additional charge. The skill simplifies this evaluation and the setup, and guides your coding agent through the following best practices before changing anything:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;A fit check before any spend.&lt;/strong&gt; The agent reads your GPU fleet with a read-only &lt;code&gt;describe-instances&lt;/code&gt; call and counts Elastic Fabric Adapter (EFA) interfaces. It asks only for what it can’t see: engine, model, tensor-parallel (TP) size, KV data type, server price, and how often prompts repeat. It stops and explains when something doesn’t fit.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Numbers from your workload.&lt;/strong&gt; For short prompts, loading KV state can take longer than computing it again. Above a certain prompt length (the cutover point), the read wins. That cutover and how often your prompts repeat decide whether the cache is worth its price. The agent estimates savings from your own traffic with KVTrace, at no additional charge, and stops if they don’t cover the cache’s cost. Every number says where it came from.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Manage configuration across inference servers with the stack you already run.&lt;/strong&gt; The agent adds the LMCache connector to your existing vLLM or SGLang engine, including vLLM workers under serving frameworks such as llm-d and Ray Serve. It keeps your routing and the engine’s settings and sets the servers in your inference fleet to one KV ID mechanism (hash and seed) and an explicit KV data type. Matching settings avoid key mismatches, where each server writes KV state the others can’t find.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Implements best practices.&lt;/strong&gt; Before production traffic, the agent empties the local tiers and checks that two cache-loaded runs give the same output, because a layout mismatch can produce healthy hit counters and wrong answers. The skill guides the agent to seek explicit approval for high-impact operations such as creating, changing, or deleting resources.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="prerequisites"&gt;Prerequisites&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;These flows, from estimating benefits to setup, require the following:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;A coding agent that supports Agent Skills, such as Kiro or Claude Code.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;GPU instances that support EFA RDMA write, such as p5.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;LMCache 0.5.8rc1 or later and vLLM 0.11.1 or later (SGLang 0.5.13 or later).&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;ElastiCache for Valkey 9.1 or later, on cache.i8ge.48xlarge nodes (if setting up the cache).&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="flow-1-can-distributed-kv-caching-help"&gt;Flow 1: Can distributed KV caching help?&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;We gave the agent this prompt: &lt;em&gt;“My vLLM fleet serves a legal assistant with 64K-token contexts on p5 instances. Returning conversations take 13 seconds for the first token because the GPU recomputes the full conversation history every turn. How do I fix this?”&lt;/em&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The agent matches the request to the skill and, in Kiro, asks for approval before loading it. The answer identifies that the latency was caused by cold prefill, and three things in the answer matter for a real deployment. First, it checks for fixes that need no new infrastructure. If you turned off prefix caching, turning it back on can speed up back-to-back turns. If the GPU prefix cache hit rate is already above 90% with session affinity, you might not need a shared KV cache. Second, it identifies constraints specific to the legal AI assistant. A shared KV cache can let one tenant tell from response times whether another tenant sent the same text. Cases that must stay isolated get separate caches, and tenants that share a cache add their own ID to the KV hash. Third, it doesn’t guess the size. It asks for the model and TP size, KV data type, replica count and routing, and how long users wait between turns.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/20/ML-22449-1.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/20/ML-22449-1.png" alt="Diagram of the agent’s diagnosis and the proposed distributed KV caching architecture for the legal assistant" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 1: The diagnosis and proposed architecture for the legal assistant&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="flow-2-estimating-savings"&gt;Flow 2: Estimating savings&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;To size the savings, we gave the agent an example fleet of 8 p5 instances that serve 64,000-token prompts for a multi-turn retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) assistant. The agent looked up the GPU and cache node prices and derived the prefill rate and KV size from benchmarks. Finally, the agent recommended two cache.i8ge.48xlarge nodes for distributed KV caching. The following figure shows the agent’s summary and the inputs it used.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/20/ML-22449-2.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/20/ML-22449-2.png" alt="The agent’s cost summary showing the GPU and cache node prices it looked up and the two recommended cache.i8ge.48xlarge nodes" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 2: The agent’s summary and inputs. It looked up both prices in the AWS price list and explains how it arrived at the recompute figure&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;We didn’t provide a cache hit rate, which is the share of recomputed tokens the cache would serve. The agent therefore presents scenarios and recommends a KVTrace run to identify the cache hit rate. KVTrace &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/database/announcing-distributed-kv-caching-for-self-hosted-llms-with-amazon-elasticache/#savings-for-your-workload" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;records the KV cache events&lt;/a&gt; from vLLM or SGLang. It then replays them offline against a shared KV cache of each candidate size. The recorder never writes token IDs or prompt text. Traces hold block hashes and token counts. Block hashes come from token content, so pass &lt;code&gt;--anonymize-key-file&lt;/code&gt; when your prompts are sensitive. The skill records on every GPU server for a representative window, such as a full business day. The agent also notes that freed GPU time turns into dollars only if you run fewer servers or serve more traffic on the same ones.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/20/ML-22449-3.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/20/ML-22449-3.png" alt="KVTrace report showing net savings at four cache hit shares, TTFT benchmarks, and the recommended shard count" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 3: The top of a KVTrace report from synthetic demo traffic. It recommends the smallest capacity that reaches 90% of the savings ceiling.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="flow-3-setting-up-a-distributed-kv-cache"&gt;Flow 3: Setting up a distributed KV cache&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;We prompted the agent with: “Set up a shared L2 KV cache for LLM inference, starting from scratch.” Before it creates anything, the agent requests your confirmation to set up the suggested cache node count in your Availability Zone, at the estimated price. Once confirmed, it creates the right security group with EFA permissions, and a Valkey 9.1 cluster that uses a custom parameter group with caching eviction policies. LMCache gets &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonElastiCache/latest/dg/distributed-kv-caching-security.html#distributed-kv-caching-security.lmcache-user" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;its own cache user&lt;/a&gt;, with the password in &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/secrets-manager/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Secrets Manager&lt;/a&gt;. The agent prepares the GPU servers and starts one LMCache server per GPU server. Then it checks the cluster with a small test object and confirms that repeated prompts load their KV state from the cache over Remote Direct Memory Access (RDMA). On a GPU fleet, the skill checks the measured L2 share against the KVTrace prediction. For the full setup with every command, see &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonElastiCache/latest/dg/distributed-kv-caching-getting-started.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Getting started with distributed KV caching&lt;/a&gt; in the Amazon ElastiCache documentation.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="flow-4-troubleshooting"&gt;Flow 4: Troubleshooting&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;blockquote&gt;
 &lt;p&gt;Prompt: “My LMCache server connects to the ElastiCache cluster and BLOB.RDMA_HELLO succeeds, but actual data transfers are failing. INFO largeobj shows rdma_write_errors climbing. What’s wrong?”&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/blockquote&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Following the skill’s troubleshooting guide, the agent reasons from the symptom pattern and identifies the likely cause as a security group that blocks EFA traffic between its own members. It provides you commands to check and fix the rule.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/20/ML-22449-4.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/20/ML-22449-4.png" alt="The agent’s troubleshooting answer: the most likely cause is a missing self-referencing egress rule. It explains why HELLO succeeds while RDMA transfers fail, gives a describe-security-groups command to check the rules and authorize-security-group-egress and ingress commands to fix them, and lists four checks to run if the rules are already correct." width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 4: The agent explains the symptom, then gives commands to check and fix the security group&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="get-started"&gt;Get started&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;You can get the &lt;a href="https://github.com/aws/agent-toolkit-for-aws/tree/main/skills/specialized-skills/database-skills/distributed-kv-caching" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;distributed KV caching&lt;/a&gt; skill or the AWS MCP Server from the &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/products/developer-tools/agent-toolkit-for-aws/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Agent Toolkit for AWS&lt;/a&gt;. Then describe your problem or ask your AI agent to cut costs and latency on your GPU fleet to get started. To learn more about distributed KV caching with ElastiCache and the performance benchmarks see &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/database/announcing-distributed-kv-caching-for-self-hosted-llms-with-amazon-elasticache/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;the AWS blog&lt;/a&gt;. You can see getting started with the AWS Command Line Interface (AWS CLI) at &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonElastiCache/latest/dg/distributed-kv-caching-getting-started.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon ElastiCache documentation&lt;/a&gt; and learn more about KVTrace at the &lt;a href="https://github.com/aws-samples/amazon-elasticache-samples/tree/main/kvtrace" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;GitHub repo&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;hr style="width: 100%"&gt;
