		<item>
		<title>Amazon SageMaker AI now detects latency drift, idle GPUs, and missed optimizations on your inference endpoints</title>
		<link>https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/amazon-sagemaker-ai-now-detects-latency-drift-idle-gpus-and-missed-optimizations-on-your-inference-endpoints/</link>
		
		<dc:creator><![CDATA[Hazim Qudah]]></dc:creator>
		<pubDate>Thu, 19 Nov 2026 21:28:18 +0000</pubDate>
				<category><![CDATA[Advanced (300)]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Amazon SageMaker AI]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Announcements]]></category>
		<guid isPermaLink="false">bd2756a23322a6c423c6a0c059236125af9e54b9</guid>

					<description>Amazon SageMaker AI now monitors your generative AI inference endpoints by default and tells you when a change could improve performance or lower cost. It tracks latency, GPU utilization, and serving configuration, then sends a finding through AWS Health with a one-click path to benchmark alternatives in SageMaker Studio before you apply any change.</description>
										<content:encoded>&lt;p&gt;Every inference endpoint is well tuned the day it goes into production. Teams choose an instance type, serving configuration, and scaling settings that suit the model and its traffic at launch. With the endpoint serving smoothly, they understandably move on to the next priority. The workload, however, rarely stays still. Traffic patterns evolve, prompts grow longer, serving engines such as vLLM ship new releases, SageMaker inference develops new optimization techniques, and new fine-tuned checkpoints change the resources your model needs. Over time, a well-tuned endpoint quietly drifts. Latency and time to first token edge upward. A configuration that was never quite ideal keeps missing available performance gains. A GPU provisioned for demand that never materialized sits idle week after week. Because nothing has failed, no alarm sounds, and the drift goes unnoticed until users feel it or the bill reflects it.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/sagemaker/ai/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon SageMaker AI&lt;/a&gt; now monitors your generative AI inference endpoints and tells you when a change could improve performance or lower cost by using &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/amazon-sagemaker-ai-now-supports-optimized-generative-ai-inference-recommendations/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Generative AI optimized inference recommendations&lt;/a&gt; feature with Agentic experience in &amp;nbsp;&lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/sagemaker/latest/dg/studio-updated-jl.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;SageMaker JupyterLab&lt;/a&gt;. Monitoring is on by default for SageMaker AI inference endpoints using vLLM serving containers and GPU instances, with no setup required, and you can opt out of endpoints. SageMaker AI looks only at operational signals: latency, GPU utilization, and the endpoint’s serving configuration. It does not read your requests or responses. From those signals, it surfaces three kinds of findings:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Latency drift:&lt;/strong&gt; A week-over-week rise in median (p50) end-to-end latency or time to first token.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Unused capacity:&lt;/strong&gt; GPUs you pay for that sit mostly idle.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Configuration gaps:&lt;/strong&gt; A serving container that’s missing newer performance improvements or security patches, or an optimization such as speculative decoding or GPU kernel tuning that your model supports but the endpoint doesn’t use.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;SageMaker AI sends a finding through AWS Health, with one notification per endpoint that covers that endpoint’s findings. When a notification arrives, you can act on it in one place. It opens the SageMaker AI agentic experience in Amazon SageMaker Studio with the endpoint, and its recent traffic already filled in. From there, you run inference recommendations to benchmark your current configuration against alternatives and compare them side by side on response time, throughput, and cost per million tokens. That turns a judgment call into a decision backed by data. Benchmarks run on separate compute, and nothing changes on your endpoint until you choose to apply a result.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;In this post, we show how SageMaker AI monitors your endpoints and how to read the notifications it sends through AWS Health. We then show how to go from a notification to a benchmarked decision in SageMaker Studio. We walk through three example scenarios, show how to apply or dismiss a recommendation safely, and explain how to opt an endpoint out of monitoring.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="solution-overview"&gt;Solution overview&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;SageMaker AI inference recommendations follow a four-step loop: observe, notify, recommend, and decide. Each step is designed so that you stay in control, and your production endpoint isn’t changed without your explicit action.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ol type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Observe –&lt;/strong&gt; Monitoring of latency and capacity usage is on by default for eligible endpoints. It doesn’t read your requests or responses, and it carries no additional GPU cost.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Notify –&lt;/strong&gt;&amp;nbsp;When there is something worth acting on, you receive a plain language notification in AWS Health, plus email, explaining what was observed, why it matters, and a recommended next step.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Recommend (opt in) –&lt;/strong&gt;&amp;nbsp;One selection opens the SageMaker AI agentic experience in SageMaker Studio, prefilled with the endpoint and its observed traffic, so you can benchmark alternatives on separate compute.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Decide –&lt;/strong&gt;&amp;nbsp;Compare response time, throughput, and cost side by side. Production is not changed until you apply a result.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The feature produces a few types of findings. The following table summarizes what triggers each one and what gets tested when you choose to benchmark.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;table border="1px" width="100%" cellpadding="10px"&gt; 
 &lt;tbody&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Finding&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;What triggers it&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;What a benchmark tests&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Latency drift&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Response time moves relative to the endpoint’s week-over-week trends&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Tensor parallelism settings and alternate instance types or sizes&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Underutilized GPUs&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;GPU utilization stays well below capacity over the window&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Cheaper instance types and smaller sizes at observed peak traffic&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Speculative decoding not enabled&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Model and container support it, but it is off (configuration based)&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Current configuration compared to speculative decoding enabled&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Serving container&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;The endpoint’s serving container has a newer version with a performance improvement and security patches for critical and high finding Common Vulnerabilities and Exposures (CVEs).&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Current compared to updated container&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Untuned GPU kernels&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Kernel configuration is not tuned for the model and hardware&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Default compared to tuned kernels&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
 &lt;/tbody&gt; 
&lt;/table&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="prerequisites"&gt;Prerequisites&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Monitoring itself needs no setup, it is on by default. The following prerequisites apply when you choose to act on a finding by running a benchmark and, optionally, applying a change:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ol type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Access to AWS Health and the AWS User Notifications console.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;A SageMaker Studio domain and AWS Identity and Access Management (IAM) permissions to run inference recommendation jobs and update endpoints.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Sufficient service quota for the benchmark instances you want to test.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Cost note:&lt;/strong&gt; Monitoring has no GPU cost. Benchmarks use standard SageMaker AI charges, or your machine learning (ML) reservation if you have one.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="understand-a-finding-notification"&gt;Understand a finding notification&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;You receive notifications in AWS Health on the console and by email and each one follows the same structure, so it’s quick to scan:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ol type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Endpoint:&lt;/strong&gt; Which endpoint the finding applies to.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;What we noticed:&lt;/strong&gt; The observation, stated plainly, with estimates clearly labeled as estimates.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Why it matters:&lt;/strong&gt; The impact on latency, capacity, or cost.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Recommended action:&lt;/strong&gt; A single selection into the agentic experience in SageMaker Studio to investigate.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Notifications report observations and label estimates. No benchmark has run at this point. A representative notification reads as follows:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;table border="1px" width="100%" cellpadding="10px"&gt; 
 &lt;tbody&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt; &lt;p&gt;Subject: [Action may be required] SageMaker AI inference improvements for your endpoint&lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt;SageMaker AI tracks the latency and capacity usage of your inference endpoints by default, and found improvements for one of them. It does not read your requests or responses.&lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt;&lt;em&gt;What we noticed: Over the past week, time to first token on this endpoint increased compared with the week before.&lt;/em&gt;&lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt;Recommended action: Open SageMaker Studio to benchmark alternatives on separate compute. Your production endpoint is not changed.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
 &lt;/tbody&gt; 
&lt;/table&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Cadence&lt;/strong&gt;: Each endpoint gets one notification that covers the applicable findings. You get at most one notification per endpoint at a time, covering all its findings, and SageMaker AI limits how many an account receives at once. Notifications are AWS Region specific, so you see an endpoint’s findings when you’re signed in to that endpoint’s Region.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="walkthrough-from-notification-to-decision"&gt;Walkthrough: From notification to decision&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;In this section, we review a few scenarios of endpoint optimizations that will trigger findings to be reported.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="scenario-1-catch-week-over-week-latency-regression"&gt;Scenario 1: Catch week-over-week latency regression&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;A team runs a chat assistant on a single SageMaker AI endpoint. They add retrieval to improve answer quality, so every request now carries pages of retrieved context and prompts get much longer. Nothing breaks, and no alarm they set trips, but responses start more slowly.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;SageMaker AI compares the endpoint’s median (p50) time to first token and median end-to-end latency with the previous week. When either one rises enough over the week, it flags the change. Because the comparison is against the endpoint’s own history, the team doesn’t need to set a threshold in advance.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Notification:&lt;/strong&gt; AWS Health reports that time to first token increased relative to the prior week.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Studio session:&lt;/strong&gt; One selection opens the agentic experience, prefilled with the endpoint and its observed traffic.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Benchmark:&lt;/strong&gt; The session tests tensor parallelism settings and other instance types or sizes on separate compute.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Decision:&lt;/strong&gt; The team compares latency, throughput, and cost, then applies the winning configuration to one variant.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The following screenshot shows an AWS Health event for a latency drift finding. The message reports that median time to first token rose about 200 ms week over week, and the Recommended actions pane links to SageMaker AI to run inference recommendations.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/17/ML-22416-1-1.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/17/ML-22416-1-1.png" alt="AWS Health latency drift event showing time to first token rose about 200 ms week over week" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 1: AWS Health event for a latency drift finding, with a link to run inference recommendations in SageMaker AI&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="scenario-2-turn-on-an-optimization-your-traffic-can-benefit-from"&gt;Scenario 2: Turn on an optimization your traffic can benefit from&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;A platform team hosts an internal code-completion model on a SageMaker AI endpoint. They deployed it with default serving settings and never revisited them. The model and serving container support speculative decoding, a technique for faster responses, but it isn’t enabled. SageMaker AI flags this as an opportunity. Whether it helps depends on the endpoint’s traffic, such as how many requests run concurrently and how long prompts and responses are. The notification recommends running inference recommendations, which benchmarks speculative decoding against the endpoint’s recent traffic and shows whether it’s worth turning on.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Notification:&lt;/strong&gt; AWS Health reports that a technique designed to make responses faster is supported but not enabled.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Studio session:&lt;/strong&gt; The agentic experience opens with the endpoint configuration loaded.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Benchmark:&lt;/strong&gt; The session compares the current configuration with speculative decoding enabled. Results may differ at peak compared to off-peak traffic.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Decision:&lt;/strong&gt; If the gain holds at the traffic that matters, the team applies it. Otherwise, they keep the current configuration.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The following screenshot shows an AWS Health event for a speculative decoding finding. The message reports that the model and serving container support speculative decoding, but the endpoint doesn’t use it. The Recommended actions pane links to SageMaker AI to run inference recommendations.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/17/ML-22416-2-1.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/17/ML-22416-2-1.png" alt="AWS Health event showing the model supports speculative decoding but the endpoint does not use it" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 2: AWS Health event for a speculative decoding finding, with a link to run inference recommendations in SageMaker AI&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The notification explains that speculative decoding can make responses faster, and that the benchmark compares response time with and without it on the endpoint’s recent traffic.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="scenario-3-right-size-underutilized-gpus"&gt;Scenario 3: Right-size underutilized GPUs&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;A summarization endpoint was sized for a launch that drew a fraction of the planned audience, so its GPUs sit mostly idle for a week. This scenario also shows a case where the right decision may be to make no change at all.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ol type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Notification:&lt;/strong&gt; AWS Health reports that the endpoint paid for GPU capacity that largely went unused.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Studio session:&lt;/strong&gt; The agentic experience opens with the endpoint and its observed peak traffic.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Benchmark:&lt;/strong&gt; The session tests cheaper instance types and smaller sizes at observed peak traffic.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Decision:&lt;/strong&gt; The team weighs the monthly savings against the headroom they want for planned growth and may decide to keep the current size.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The following screenshot shows an AWS Health event for an unused capacity finding. The message reports that for most of the week, even the endpoint’s busiest GPU used 12 percent or less of its capacity. The Recommended actions pane links to SageMaker AI to run inference recommendations.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/17/ML-22416-3-1.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/17/ML-22416-3-1.png" alt="AWS Health unused capacity event showing the busiest GPU used 12% or less of its capacity for most of the week" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 3: AWS Health event for an unused capacity finding, with a link to run inference recommendations in SageMaker AI&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;You can choose &lt;strong&gt;Open in SageMaker AI&lt;/strong&gt; from the recommended actions as shown in the screenshot above to apply or dismiss a recommendation. Lets walk through this in next section.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="apply-or-dismiss-a-recommendation"&gt;Apply or dismiss a recommendation&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;You stay in control throughout, and nothing deploys automatically. When you choose &lt;strong&gt;Open&lt;/strong&gt; in SageMaker AI from a notification’s recommended actions, &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/sagemaker/ai/studio/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon SageMaker Studio&lt;/a&gt; opens a JupyterLab space and shows the prompt that will run in the agentic chat. The following screenshot shows the prompt for a latency drift finding on an endpoint that also runs an older vLLM serving container:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;img loading="lazy" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-145702" src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/19/ml-22416-edit1.png" alt="" width="832" height="238"&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;When you choose &lt;strong&gt;Run&lt;/strong&gt;, the agentic experience opens in a chat panel in JupyterLab space in SageMaker Studio. It summarizes the finding in plain language, restates what you asked it to do, and checks with you before it takes any action. The following screenshot shows the agent summarizing the findings and asking to continue:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;img loading="lazy" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-145703" src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/19/ml-22416-edit2.png" alt="" width="360" height="774"&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;After you confirm, the agent generates a notebook that creates an inference recommendation job for the endpoint. The notebook uses the SageMaker Python SDK, takes the model from the endpoint and the traffic profile from its metrics, and tags the job with the AWS Health event so you can trace the results back to the finding. Each step is a separate notebook cell, so you can review or edit it before you run it.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The following screenshot shows the notebook cells that record the job details and start a recommendation job across three candidate instance types.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;img loading="lazy" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-145704" src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/19/ml-22416-edit3.png" alt="" width="756" height="552"&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;When the job completes, you can compare your current configuration with the alternatives on response time, throughput, and cost. When you are ready to act on a benchmarked result, we recommend a safe rollout pattern:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Review the proposed change before applying it, including the configuration difference and the benchmarked latency, throughput, and cost.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Apply to one production variant first and keep the current configuration available for rollback.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Holding or dismissing a finding is a normal outcome. The notification stays in your AWS Health event history.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="manage-monitoring-and-notifications"&gt;Manage monitoring and notifications&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;You can manage monitoring and how you are notified:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ol type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Manage how you receive notifications in the AWS User Notifications console.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Optionally, schedule recurring recommendations, for example monthly.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;What is and is not collected:&lt;/strong&gt; Monitoring reads aggregated latency and capacity telemetry at the endpoint level. It doesn’t read your request or response content, and it carries no GPU cost.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Opting out of monitoring:&lt;/strong&gt; If you don’t want SageMaker AI to monitor an endpoint, set the &lt;code&gt;OPT_OUT_TRACKING&lt;/code&gt; environment variable, with a value of your choice, on the model’s container. You set it in the &lt;code&gt;Environment&lt;/code&gt; map when you call &lt;code&gt;CreateModel&lt;/code&gt;, either on &lt;code&gt;PrimaryContainer&lt;/code&gt; or on another container in the model.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;If a container on the endpoint carries this variable, SageMaker AI collects no monitoring telemetry for that endpoint, including engine metrics, GPU metrics, and startup configuration details. As a result, it sends no notifications for that endpoint.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="clean-up"&gt;Clean up&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;To avoid ongoing charges, clean up the resources you created while following this post:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ol type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Delete any test endpoints or resources you created for the walkthrough.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Remove benchmark artifacts in Amazon Simple Storage Service (Amazon S3), if applicable.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Revert any test configuration changes you applied.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="conclusion"&gt;Conclusion&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;With SageMaker AI inference recommendations now monitoring your endpoints automatically, optimization moves from a point-in-time snapshot to an ongoing signal.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Your endpoints keep telling you when they have drifted, when they are missing an improvement, or when they are paying for idle capacity. A single selection takes you to a benchmarked, side-by-side decision that you control.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;For more information on how to get started, visit &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/sagemaker/ai/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon SageMaker AI&lt;/a&gt; and see the &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/sagemaker/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon SageMaker AI documentation&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;

					<description>For organizations hiring hundreds or thousands of people, every interview presents the same challenge: How do you give candidates a consistent opportunity to demonstrate their skills while gathering the evidence needed to make informed hiring decisions?&amp;nbsp; Structured behavioral interviews offer a research-backed approach to this challenge. They use consistent, job-related questions and clear scoring criteria […]</description>
										<content:encoded>&lt;p&gt;&lt;span class="TextRun SCXW252313659 BCX0" lang="EN-US" xml:lang="EN-US" data-contrast="auto"&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW252313659 BCX0"&gt;For organizations hiring &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW252313659 BCX0"&gt;hundreds or &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW252313659 BCX0"&gt;thousands of people, every interview presents the same challenge: How do you give candidates a consistent opportunity to &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW252313659 BCX0"&gt;demonstrate&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW252313659 BCX0"&gt; their skills while gathering the evidence needed to make informed hiring decisions?&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="EOP Selected SCXW252313659 BCX0" data-ccp-props="{&amp;quot;201341983&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559739&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559740&amp;quot;:278}"&gt;&amp;nbsp;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;span class="TextRun SCXW193008649 BCX0" lang="EN-US" xml:lang="EN-US" data-contrast="auto"&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW193008649 BCX0"&gt;Structured behavioral interviews offer a research-backed approach to this challenge. They use consistent, job-related questions and clear scoring criteria to give candidates an opportunity to &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW193008649 BCX0"&gt;demonstrate&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW193008649 BCX0"&gt; their skills. Decades of research in industrial-organizational (I-O) psychology have shown that structured interviews help organizations evaluate candidates and predict job performance (Schmidt &amp;amp; Hunter, 1998; Sackett et al., 2022). Yet delivering &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW193008649 BCX0"&gt;structured interviews&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW193008649 BCX0"&gt; at scale requires &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW193008649 BCX0"&gt;significant time&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW193008649 BCX0"&gt; and &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW193008649 BCX0"&gt;expertise&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW193008649 BCX0"&gt; to develop&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW193008649 BCX0"&gt; competencies, &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW193008649 BCX0"&gt;create q&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW193008649 BCX0"&gt;uestions, train interviewers, and evaluate responses consistently&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW193008649 BCX0"&gt;.&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="EOP Selected SCXW193008649 BCX0" data-ccp-props="{&amp;quot;201341983&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559739&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559740&amp;quot;:278}"&gt;&amp;nbsp;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;span data-contrast="auto"&gt;&lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/products/connect/talent/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Connect Talent&lt;/a&gt; &lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW175523192 BCX0"&gt;brings established hiring science to AI-led interviews at scale. &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW175523192 BCX0"&gt;Connect Talent &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW175523192 BCX0"&gt;uses structured questions, adaptive follow-ups, and evidence-based scoring to help organizations gather consistent, job-related information while giving more candidates the opportunity to demonstrate their strengths.&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;span class="TextRun SCXW66502508 BCX0" lang="EN-US" xml:lang="EN-US" data-contrast="auto"&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW66502508 BCX0"&gt;We built Connect Talent around three core design principles and evaluated how well the service &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun ContextualSpellingAndGrammarErrorV2Themed SCXW66502508 BCX0"&gt;applies&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW66502508 BCX0"&gt; them through &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW66502508 BCX0"&gt;a combination of &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW66502508 BCX0"&gt;internal research and independent expert review. &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW66502508 BCX0"&gt;The &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW66502508 BCX0"&gt;findings offer a closer look at the science behind &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW66502508 BCX0"&gt;Connect Talent’s &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW66502508 BCX0"&gt;AI-le&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW66502508 BCX0"&gt;d,&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW66502508 BCX0"&gt; structured interviewing.&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="EOP Selected SCXW66502508 BCX0" data-ccp-props="{&amp;quot;201341983&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559739&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559740&amp;quot;:278}"&gt;&amp;nbsp;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2&gt;&lt;b&gt;&lt;span data-contrast="auto"&gt;Three core design principles&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;h3&gt;&lt;b&gt;&lt;span data-contrast="auto"&gt;1. Build interviews around job-related evidence&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;span class="TextRun SCXW227730746 BCX0" lang="EN-US" xml:lang="EN-US" data-contrast="auto"&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW227730746 BCX0"&gt;Connect Talent builds each interview around the competencies &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW227730746 BCX0"&gt;required&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW227730746 BCX0"&gt; for the role. The service uses job requirements and &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun ContextualSpellingAndGrammarErrorV2Themed SCXW227730746 BCX0"&gt;established&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW227730746 BCX0"&gt; occupational frameworks to create behaviorally anchored scorecards. Each scorecard defines specific strength and concern signals, and every interview question targets the behaviors it aims to uncover.&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="EOP Selected SCXW227730746 BCX0" data-ccp-props="{&amp;quot;201341983&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559739&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559740&amp;quot;:278}"&gt;&amp;nbsp;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;span class="TextRun SCXW128739577 BCX0" lang="EN-US" xml:lang="EN-US" data-contrast="auto"&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW128739577 BCX0"&gt;The AI interviewer also adapts to each response. After every answer, it checks whether the candidate has provided enough evidence for the competency being assessed. When details are missing, it asks targeted follow-up questions. This approach preserves the structure of the interview while giving candidates more opportunities to explain their experience.&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="EOP Selected SCXW128739577 BCX0" data-ccp-props="{&amp;quot;201341983&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559739&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559740&amp;quot;:278}"&gt;&amp;nbsp;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3&gt;&lt;b&gt;&lt;span data-contrast="auto"&gt;2. Separate interviewing from scoring&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;span class="TextRun SCXW256134426 BCX0" lang="EN-US" xml:lang="EN-US" data-contrast="auto"&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW256134426 BCX0"&gt;Connect Talent separates the interview conversation from the evaluation. The AI interviewer gathers evidence but does not assign scores. Afterward, a separate evaluation process &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW256134426 BCX0"&gt;identifies&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW256134426 BCX0"&gt; which behaviors the candidate &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW256134426 BCX0"&gt;demonstrated&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW256134426 BCX0"&gt; and cites the supporting transcript passa&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun CommentStart CommentHighlightPipeRest CommentHighlightRest SCXW256134426 BCX0"&gt;ges.&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="EOP Selected SCXW256134426 BCX0" data-ccp-props="{&amp;quot;201341983&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559739&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559740&amp;quot;:278}"&gt;&amp;nbsp;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;span class="TextRun SCXW48084853 BCX0" lang="EN-US" xml:lang="EN-US" data-contrast="auto"&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun CommentHighlightRest SCXW48084853 BCX0"&gt;The service calculates each competency score from 1 to 5 &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun CommentHighlightPipeRest SCXW48084853 BCX0"&gt;using a fixed formula. When the transcript lacks sufficient evidence, Connect Talent flags the competency instead of producing a score. Hiring teams can review the evidence behind each rating, and missing evidence does not automatically count against the candidate.&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="EOP Selected SCXW48084853 BCX0" data-ccp-props="{&amp;quot;201341983&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559739&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559740&amp;quot;:278}"&gt;&amp;nbsp;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3&gt;&lt;b&gt;&lt;span data-contrast="auto"&gt;3. Build fairness protections into the design&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;span class="TextRun SCXW206140517 BCX0" lang="EN-US" xml:lang="EN-US" data-contrast="auto"&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW206140517 BCX0"&gt;Connect Talent evaluates transcribed candidate responses without using video, voice tone, or physical appearance to calculate competency scores. The service also excludes candidate identity information from scoring and evaluates each competency separately. These safeguards help &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun ContextualSpellingAndGrammarErrorV2Themed SCXW206140517 BCX0"&gt;focus&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW206140517 BCX0"&gt; assessments on job-related behaviors and limit the influence of unrelated information.&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="EOP Selected SCXW206140517 BCX0" data-ccp-props="{&amp;quot;201341983&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559739&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559740&amp;quot;:278}"&gt;&amp;nbsp;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3&gt;&lt;b&gt;&lt;span data-contrast="auto"&gt;What the research shows&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW13849229 BCX0"&gt;To understand how well the&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW13849229 BCX0"&gt;se design principles&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW13849229 BCX0"&gt; work in practice, we combined independent I-O psychology evaluations with internal research. We examined whether the service generates job-relevant interview content, &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW13849229 BCX0"&gt;identifies&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW13849229 BCX0"&gt; behavioral evidence in line with expert assessments, gathers more complete responses through follow-up questions, and distinguishes between levels of &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW13849229 BCX0"&gt;demonstrated&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW13849229 BCX0"&gt; competency.&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2&gt;&lt;b&gt;&lt;span data-contrast="auto"&gt;Connect Talent keeps interview questions focused on job-relevant skills&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/b&gt;&lt;span data-ccp-props="{&amp;quot;201341983&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559739&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559740&amp;quot;:278}"&gt;&amp;nbsp;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;span class="TextRun SCXW207373011 BCX0" lang="EN-US" xml:lang="EN-US" data-contrast="auto"&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW207373011 BCX0"&gt;The quality of a structured interview starts with the skills it measures. Candidates need questions that reflect the requirements of the role, and hiring teams need confidence that the interview assesses those skills.&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="EOP Selected SCXW207373011 BCX0" data-ccp-props="{&amp;quot;201341983&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559739&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559740&amp;quot;:278}"&gt;&amp;nbsp;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;span class="TextRun SCXW20718154 BCX0" lang="EN-US" xml:lang="EN-US" data-contrast="auto"&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW20718154 BCX0"&gt;Amazon engaged an independent I-O psychology firm to evaluate Connect Talent’s AI-generated interview content. The firm reviewed the service’s baseline output before human editing and examined how well its competencies, questions&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW20718154 BCX0"&gt;, and rubrics&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW20718154 BCX0"&gt; aligned with job requirements.&lt;/span&gt; &lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW20718154 BCX0"&gt;The evaluation found that Connect Talent &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW20718154 BCX0"&gt;retained&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW20718154 BCX0"&gt; critical job information in 100% of the job descriptions reviewed. Of the competencies generated, 91% were clearly traceable to the job description. The firm’s psychologists also found that 98% of interview questions targeted the intended competencies, &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW20718154 BCX0"&gt;and &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW20718154 BCX0"&gt;94% of &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW20718154 BCX0"&gt;rubric &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW20718154 BCX0"&gt;signals clearly measured the intended competency.&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="EOP Selected SCXW20718154 BCX0" data-ccp-props="{&amp;quot;201341983&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559739&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559740&amp;quot;:278}"&gt;&amp;nbsp;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;span class="TextRun SCXW78714446 BCX0" lang="EN-US" xml:lang="EN-US" data-contrast="auto"&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun CommentStart CommentHighlightPipeRest CommentHighlightRest SCXW78714446 BCX0"&gt;These findings show that Connect Talent generated job-relevant interview content that met independent expert criteria in &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun CommentHighlightRest SCXW78714446 BCX0"&gt;a vast majority of&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun CommentHighlightRest SCXW78714446 BCX0"&gt; cases.&lt;/span&gt; &lt;span class="NormalTextRun CommentHighlightPipeRest SCXW78714446 BCX0"&gt;Hiring teams can then review, select, and edit the competencies, scorecards, and questions before using them. This human oversight gives organizations control over the interview process while helping them apply structured hiring practices at &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun ContextualSpellingAndGrammarErrorV2Themed SCXW78714446 BCX0"&gt;scale&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW78714446 BCX0"&gt;.&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="EOP Selected SCXW78714446 BCX0" data-ccp-props="{&amp;quot;201341983&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559739&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559740&amp;quot;:278}"&gt;&amp;nbsp;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3&gt;&lt;b&gt;&lt;span data-contrast="auto"&gt;Connect Talent’s candidate scoring matched independent expert decisions more than 89% of the time&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/b&gt;&lt;span data-ccp-props="{&amp;quot;201341983&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559739&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559740&amp;quot;:278}"&gt;&amp;nbsp;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;span class="TextRun SCXW101450337 BCX0" lang="EN-US" xml:lang="EN-US" data-contrast="auto"&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW101450337 BCX0"&gt;Relevant questions provide the foundation for a structured interview. &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW101450337 BCX0"&gt;The next challenge is evaluating the evidence candidates provide with consistency.&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="EOP Selected SCXW101450337 BCX0" data-ccp-props="{&amp;quot;201341983&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559739&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559740&amp;quot;:278}"&gt;&amp;nbsp;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span data-ccp-props="{&amp;quot;201341983&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559739&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559740&amp;quot;:278}"&gt;&amp;nbsp;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;span data-contrast="auto"&gt;To examine this, an independent I-O psychology firm reviewed 36 interviews assessing whether candidates demonstrated specific strengths and concerns, creating 252 rubric signals.&amp;nbsp;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span data-ccp-props="{&amp;quot;201341983&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559739&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559740&amp;quot;:278}"&gt;&amp;nbsp;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;span class="TextRun SCXW166315638 BCX0" lang="EN-US" xml:lang="EN-US" data-contrast="auto"&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW166315638 BCX0"&gt;Connect Talent evaluated the same transcripts&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW166315638 BCX0"&gt;, &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW166315638 BCX0"&gt;reviewed &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW166315638 BCX0"&gt;hundreds of&lt;/span&gt; &lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW166315638 BCX0"&gt;rubric signals&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW166315638 BCX0"&gt;, and &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW166315638 BCX0"&gt;aligned with expert scoring decisions &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW166315638 BCX0"&gt;in &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW166315638 BCX0"&gt;89.3%&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW166315638 BCX0"&gt; of c&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW166315638 BCX0"&gt;ases&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW166315638 BCX0"&gt;.&lt;/span&gt; &lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW166315638 BCX0"&gt;Scoring alignment &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW166315638 BCX0"&gt;reached 97.6% when candidates provided clear evidence &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW166315638 BCX0"&gt;and 92.9% &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW166315638 BCX0"&gt;when &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW166315638 BCX0"&gt;only &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW166315638 BCX0"&gt;considering candidate &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW166315638 BCX0"&gt;concern signals, which identify behaviors that may indicate &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW166315638 BCX0"&gt;a poor fit for the intended role&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW166315638 BCX0"&gt;.&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW166315638 BCX0"&gt;&amp;nbsp;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="EOP Selected SCXW166315638 BCX0" data-ccp-props="{&amp;quot;201341983&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559739&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559740&amp;quot;:278}"&gt;&amp;nbsp;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;span class="TextRun SCXW226846916 BCX0" lang="EN-US" xml:lang="EN-US" data-contrast="auto"&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW226846916 BCX0"&gt;The evaluation also produced a &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun CommentStart CommentHighlightPipeRest CommentHighlightRest SCXW226846916 BCX0"&gt;Cohen’s kappa of 0.79, a statistical measure that &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun CommentHighlightRest SCXW226846916 BCX0"&gt;eliminates&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun CommentHighlightRest SCXW226846916 BCX0"&gt; random chance coincidence&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun CommentHighlightRest SCXW226846916 BCX0"&gt;.&lt;/span&gt; &lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW226846916 BCX0"&gt;A Cohen’s kappa of 0.79 is just under the standard of &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW226846916 BCX0"&gt;0&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW226846916 BCX0"&gt;.8&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW226846916 BCX0"&gt;1&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW226846916 BCX0"&gt; for almost perfect agreement&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW226846916 BCX0"&gt;. &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW226846916 BCX0"&gt;This &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW226846916 BCX0"&gt;ou&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW226846916 BCX0"&gt;tc&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW226846916 BCX0"&gt;ome &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW226846916 BCX0"&gt;indicates&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW226846916 BCX0"&gt; substantial agreement &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW226846916 BCX0"&gt;between Connect Talent and the independent expert &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW226846916 BCX0"&gt;candidate scoring.&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW226846916 BCX0"&gt;&amp;nbsp;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="EOP Selected SCXW226846916 BCX0" data-ccp-props="{&amp;quot;201341983&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559739&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559740&amp;quot;:278}"&gt;&amp;nbsp;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3&gt;&lt;b&gt;&lt;span data-contrast="auto"&gt;Follow-up questions give candidates more opportunities to demonstrate their strengths&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/b&gt;&lt;span data-ccp-props="{&amp;quot;201341983&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559739&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559740&amp;quot;:278}"&gt;&amp;nbsp;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;span data-contrast="auto"&gt;Candidates may have relevant experience but leave important details out of their first response. In a traditional one-way video interview, a candidate may receive a fixed question and have only one opportunity to answer it. Hiring teams must then evaluate the information available, even when the response leaves many important questions unanswered.&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span data-ccp-props="{&amp;quot;201341983&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559739&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559740&amp;quot;:278}"&gt;&amp;nbsp;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;span data-contrast="auto"&gt;Connect Talent uses adaptive follow-ups to gather more complete evidence. For example, a candidate might describe improving a slow system. The AI interviewer can ask what changed, which actions the candidate took, and how they measured the improvement. These questions help candidates explain their contributions and results in greater detail.&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span data-ccp-props="{&amp;quot;201341983&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559739&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559740&amp;quot;:278}"&gt;&amp;nbsp;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;span data-contrast="auto"&gt;We studied the impact of follow-up questions by comparing assessments based on a candidate’s first response with assessments based on the full interview conversation. In a preliminary evaluation, scores based only on the first response were up to 1.14 points lower on a five-point scale than scores based on the full conversation. Assessments using the full conversation also came closer to expert evaluations.&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span data-ccp-props="{&amp;quot;201341983&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559739&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559740&amp;quot;:278}"&gt;&amp;nbsp;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;span data-contrast="auto"&gt;Without follow-up questions, the scoring process identified fewer strength signals and more concern signals. In some cases, the candidate’s initial response simply lacked the information needed to demonstrate a relevant behavior. The full conversation captured more of what candidates demonstrated and gave hiring teams a more complete record of their experience.&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span data-ccp-props="{&amp;quot;201341983&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559739&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559740&amp;quot;:278}"&gt;&amp;nbsp;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3&gt;&lt;b&gt;&lt;span data-contrast="auto"&gt;Structured scoring helps hiring teams recognize meaningful differences&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/b&gt;&lt;span data-ccp-props="{&amp;quot;201341983&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559739&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559740&amp;quot;:278}"&gt;&amp;nbsp;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;span data-contrast="auto"&gt;Hiring teams need scores that reflect meaningful differences in demonstrated skills. Yet research has found that some AI scoring approaches concentrate ratings within a narrow range, making those differences harder to recognize (Zhu et al., 2026).&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span data-ccp-props="{&amp;quot;201341983&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559739&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559740&amp;quot;:278}"&gt;&amp;nbsp;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;span data-contrast="auto"&gt;Connect Talent uses behaviorally anchored scorecards to evaluate specific strength and concern signals. Rather than asking an AI model to assign an overall rating, the service identifies evidence for each behavior and calculates the competency score using a predefined formula.&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span data-ccp-props="{&amp;quot;201341983&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559739&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559740&amp;quot;:278}"&gt;&amp;nbsp;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;span data-contrast="auto"&gt;Consider the competency “collaboration”. A general AI scoring approach might evaluate a candidate’s overall collaboration skills based on the interview transcript. Connect Talent examines specific behaviors, such as whether the candidate sought input from colleagues before making a decision. Each finding contributes to the final score.&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span data-ccp-props="{&amp;quot;201341983&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559739&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559740&amp;quot;:278}"&gt;&amp;nbsp;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;span data-contrast="auto"&gt;We evaluated this approach across hundreds of expert-rated interview assessments. Connect Talent’s structured scoring used all five points of the rating scale, while holistic LLM-as-judge scoring concentrated its ratings within the middle three points.&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span data-ccp-props="{&amp;quot;201341983&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559739&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559740&amp;quot;:278}"&gt;&amp;nbsp;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;span data-contrast="auto"&gt;This wider distribution allows the scoring system to represent more levels of demonstrated competency. The behaviorally anchored scorecards also help hiring teams understand the evidence behind each rating.&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span data-ccp-props="{&amp;quot;201341983&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559739&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559740&amp;quot;:278}"&gt;&amp;nbsp;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2&gt;&lt;b&gt;&lt;span data-contrast="auto"&gt;Bringing hiring science to AI-led interviews&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/b&gt;&lt;span data-ccp-props="{&amp;quot;201341983&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559739&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559740&amp;quot;:278}"&gt;&amp;nbsp;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;span class="TextRun SCXW248885655 BCX0" lang="EN-US" xml:lang="EN-US" data-contrast="auto"&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW248885655 BCX0"&gt;Connect Talent&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW248885655 BCX0"&gt; bring&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW248885655 BCX0"&gt;s&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW248885655 BCX0"&gt; the rigor of structured interviewing to more people while giving each candidate a meaningful chance to &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW248885655 BCX0"&gt;demonstrate&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW248885655 BCX0"&gt; their skills.&lt;/span&gt; &lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW248885655 BCX0"&gt;The result is a new opportunity for &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW248885655 BCX0"&gt;organizations &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW248885655 BCX0"&gt;hiring at scale: more candidates can show what they can do, and &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW248885655 BCX0"&gt;more &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="NormalTextRun SCXW248885655 BCX0"&gt;hiring teams can make decisions grounded in the evidence that matters.&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class="EOP Selected SCXW248885655 BCX0" data-ccp-props="{&amp;quot;201341983&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559739&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559740&amp;quot;:278}"&gt;&amp;nbsp;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2&gt;&lt;b&gt;&lt;span data-contrast="auto"&gt;Learn More at AWS re:Invent 2026&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/b&gt;&lt;span data-ccp-props="{&amp;quot;201341983&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559739&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559740&amp;quot;:278}"&gt;&amp;nbsp;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;span data-contrast="auto"&gt;Join us at AWS re:Invent 2026 for &lt;/span&gt;&lt;b&gt;&lt;span data-contrast="auto"&gt;BIZ332: Every Candidate, Every Time: AI-Led Structured Interviews with Amazon Connect Talent&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/b&gt;&lt;span data-contrast="auto"&gt;, an interactive Chalk Talk led by Sunitha Srivatsa and Gerald Friedland.&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span data-ccp-props="{&amp;quot;201341983&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559739&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559740&amp;quot;:278}"&gt;&amp;nbsp;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;span data-contrast="auto"&gt;The session will explore the science behind Connect Talent, including structured interviewing, adaptive follow-up questions, and evidence-based scoring.&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span data-ccp-props="{&amp;quot;201341983&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559739&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559740&amp;quot;:278}"&gt;&amp;nbsp;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/p&gt;

  &lt;h3 class="lb-h4"&gt;&lt;span aria-label="Rich text content control paragraph"&gt;&lt;span data-contrast="auto"&gt;Sunitha Srivatsa&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/h3&gt; 
  &lt;p style="overflow: hidden"&gt;&lt;span data-contrast="auto"&gt;​&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span aria-label="Rich text content control paragraph"&gt;&lt;span data-contrast="auto"&gt;​&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span data-contrast="auto"&gt;Sunitha Srivatsa is a Principal Software Development Engineer at AWS and the technical lead for Amazon Connect Talent and Amazon Connect Customer Global Resiliency. Amazon Connect Talent is an agentic AI hiring solution that uses AI-led structured interviews and behavioral evidence to help businesses hire faster and more fairly at scale. Sunitha owns the end-to-end architecture for Connect Talent, with a focus on the AI interviewer. On Global Resiliency, she leads the design of multi-region capabilities that let customers run their contact centers across multiple AWS Regions in an active-active setup. Sunitha joined Amazon in 2013 and has worked on Amazon Connect since 2020. Before that, she built products across AWS, including the Amazon Silk browser and Alexa for Business. Her specialties include system architecture, multi-region resiliency, and building services that businesses can rely on at scale. Outside work, Sunitha enjoys traveling the world with her two young kids and dabbles in yoga and dance.&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span data-contrast="auto"&gt;​&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span data-ccp-props="{&amp;quot;201341983&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559739&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559740&amp;quot;:278}"&gt;&amp;nbsp;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/p&gt;

 &lt;div class="blog-author-box" style="padding-top: 2.0em"&gt; 
  &lt;div class="blog-author-image" style="margin-right: 1.0em"&gt;
   &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/19/Gerald-Friedland.jpg"&gt;&lt;img loading="lazy" class="alignnone wp-image-145695 size-full" src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/19/Gerald-Friedland.jpg" alt="" width="449" height="449"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
  &lt;/div&gt; 
  &lt;h3 class="lb-h4"&gt;&lt;span aria-label="Rich text content control paragraph"&gt;&lt;span data-contrast="auto"&gt;Gerald Friedland&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/h3&gt; 
  &lt;p style="overflow: hidden"&gt;&lt;span aria-label="Rich text content control paragraph"&gt;&lt;span data-contrast="auto"&gt;Gerald Friedland is a Principal Applied Scientist at AWS and the science head of Amazon Connect Talent. Amazon Connect Talent is an agentic AI hiring solution that uses AI-led structured interviews and behavioral evidence to help businesses hire faster and more fairly at scale. Gerald leads the science behind the service’s high-volume AI interview systems. He also created privacy-enhanced synthetic data for AWS Clean Rooms and co-authored MITRA, Amazon’s tabular foundation model. He has more than 25 years of experience in research, engineering, and teaching. Before Connect Talent, he led science work on Amazon SageMaker. He also founded Brainome, an AutoML company, and Audeme, which built a speech recognition board for Arduino. Earlier, he was adjunct faculty at UC Berkeley, Principal Data Scientist at Lawrence Livermore National Laboratory, and a research director at the International Computer Science Institute. His specialties include information theory, machine learning, and explaining AI through first principles. He has written four books, including Information-Driven Machine Learning, and more than 200 publications, and holds a PhD in computer science from Freie Universität Berlin.&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span data-contrast="auto"&gt;​&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;span data-ccp-props="{&amp;quot;201341983&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559739&amp;quot;:0,&amp;quot;335559740&amp;quot;:278}"&gt;&amp;nbsp;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/p&gt;

			</item>
		<item>
		<title>Amazon Bedrock AgentCore harness adds Strands harness defaults, built-in tools, and lifecycle hooks</title>
		<link>https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/amazon-bedrock-agentcore-harness-adds-strands-harness-defaults-built-in-tools-and-lifecycle-hooks/</link>
		
		<dc:creator><![CDATA[Evandro Franco]]></dc:creator>
		<pubDate>Thu, 19 Nov 2026 20:01:06 +0000</pubDate>
				<category><![CDATA[Advanced (300)]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Amazon Bedrock AgentCore]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Announcements]]></category>
		<guid isPermaLink="false">83c0c30f184dfdf0ae395f9aa3d7c3629ffc28b5</guid>

					<description>Amazon Bedrock AgentCore harness now runs on the open-source Strands harness, bringing a recommended model, task-oriented defaults, automatic prompt caching, proactive context management, and new built-in tools. It also adds lifecycle hooks so you can run deterministic allow or deny checks at defined points in the agent loop.</description>
										<content:encoded>&lt;p&gt;When building an AI agent, selecting a model is only the first step. The agent still needs tools, instructions for finishing the job, a way to manage context growth in long sessions, and guardrails before it executes any consequential actions, like issuing a refund.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;We are upgrading Amazon Bedrock AgentCore harness to use Strands harness, our open-source agent harness, released earlier this year, available under an Apache 2.0 license. AgentCore harness also introduces lifecycle hooks, so you can apply your own deterministic checks and notifications at defined points in the agent loop.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;With this upgrade, agents that rely on the managed defaults get a recommended model, a task-oriented system prompt, automatic prompt caching (initially for Anthropic models on Amazon Bedrock), and proactive context management. They also get additional built-in tools for planning, web retrieval, filesystem access, context offloading, and task delegation. This gives agents greater capabilities during long tasks, allowing them to keep track of subtasks, reduce context window usage, and delegate work. With hooks, you can transform non-deterministic AI behavior into predictable, safe, and governed workflows by running deterministic code at specific lifecycle checkpoints.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;There’s no new resource type or integration to adopt. You automatically get both updates in the AgentCore harness you already use.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="strands-capabilities-on-aws"&gt;Strands capabilities, on AWS&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href="https://strandsagents.com/docs/user-guide/harness/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Strands harness&lt;/a&gt; provides an assembled agent foundation with recommended instructions, tools, and context management. &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock-agentcore/latest/devguide/harness.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock AgentCore harness&lt;/a&gt; operates that foundation as a managed AWS service. The result is a production-grade default agent that doesn’t require you to assemble and operate the harness or its infrastructure yourself.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;You do not need to provision compute, bundle and host the agent loop, manage session scaling, or operate tool-execution infrastructure. Each session runs in a secure, kernel-isolated microVM in AgentCore runtime, a capability of Amazon Bedrock AgentCore, with managed compute, scaling, and high availability.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;AgentCore achieves this by connecting the harness to the services required for production agents, including session persistence, streaming responses, usage metadata, observability, AgentCore memory, Identity, Gateway, Browser, Code Interpreter, and customer-provided containers. You retain control over the model, instructions, tools, permissions, and lifecycle policies while AgentCore manages the underlying execution environment.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="a-more-capable-managed-harness"&gt;A more capable managed harness&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Harness now provides a stronger experience when you use its managed defaults:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;A current recommended model.&lt;/strong&gt; When you omit the model, Harness uses a recommended default model that evolves as new models are released, always keeping your harnesses up to date. Explicit harness-level and per-invocation model configurations continue to take precedence.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Task-oriented instructions.&lt;/strong&gt; The managed system prompt encourages the agent to gather relevant context, use appropriate tools, continue until the task is resolved, adapt to failures or denied actions, and verify its work before reporting completion. The default system prompt improves over time to support the latest harness behavior.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Automatic prompt caching.&lt;/strong&gt; For supported models using Converse on Amazon Bedrock, Harness caches the system prompt, tool definitions, and conversation history. Repeated calls with the same prompt and tool surface can use lower-priced cache-read tokens, reducing input processing latency and cost.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Proactive context management.&lt;/strong&gt; By default, Harness summarizes older conversation turns while retaining recent context once the context window reaches a configurable threshold. Keeping the context lean this way helps Harness run at &lt;a href="https://strandsagents.com/blog/we-ran-94000-trials-to-measure-the-cost-of-harness-choice/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;about 34% lower token cost than other leading harnesses&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Context offloading.&lt;/strong&gt; Large tool results can be moved out of the immediate model context. The agent receives a preview and can retrieve the complete result when needed, reducing repeated context usage without losing access to the data.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Planning across invocations.&lt;/strong&gt; The &lt;code&gt;todo_write&lt;/code&gt; tool maintains a structured task plan that can be restored in later invocations within the same session, including after conversation summarization.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Focused delegation.&lt;/strong&gt; The &lt;code&gt;subagent&lt;/code&gt; tool delegates a self-contained task to a fresh worker and returns the result to the parent agent. The worker is internal to the invocation, not a separately managed harness resource, and shares invocation limits and your tool-call lifecycle hooks.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Web retrieval.&lt;/strong&gt; The &lt;code&gt;web_fetch&lt;/code&gt; tool retrieves content from a specified webpage to give your harness up-to-date external context.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Granular filesystem access.&lt;/strong&gt; With separate &lt;code&gt;read&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;write&lt;/code&gt;, and &lt;code&gt;edit&lt;/code&gt; tools, you can grant only the granular filesystem capabilities an agent needs.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Customer-provided Model Context Protocol (MCP) servers, AgentCore Gateway (a capability of Amazon Bedrock AgentCore), Browser, Code Interpreter, inline functions, skills, memory, and customer-provided containers continue to work through the existing harness interfaces.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="recommended-by-default-configurable-when-needed"&gt;Recommended by default, configurable when needed&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Harness manages its defaults so that agents using them receive the current AgentCore recommended experience as models, instructions, context management, and built-in capabilities improve.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Explicit configuration always takes precedence:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Configure a model ID to keep using a specific model.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Configure &lt;code&gt;systemPrompt&lt;/code&gt; to completely replace the managed system prompt.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Configure &lt;code&gt;truncation&lt;/code&gt; to keep a specific context-management policy.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;List individual &lt;code&gt;allowedTools&lt;/code&gt; selectors to maintain a fixed tool surface.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;When &lt;code&gt;allowedTools&lt;/code&gt; is omitted or set to &lt;code&gt;["*"]&lt;/code&gt;, Harness provides the current recommended built-in tool set. Because this set can gain capabilities over time, applications with a fixed security or capability boundary should list only the tools they need.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;For example, the following configuration permits read-only filesystem access and web retrieval without granting shell access or file modification:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-json"&gt;{
    "allowedTools": [
        "read",
        "web_fetch"
    ]
}&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="govern-consequential-actions-with-lifecycle-hooks"&gt;Govern consequential actions with lifecycle hooks&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;With lifecycle hooks, you can run your own deterministic code at defined points in the agent loop. For example, an AWS Lambda function can allow or deny a request or a tool call, and an Amazon Simple Notification Service (Amazon SNS) topic or Amazon EventBridge event bus can receive a notification to record activity or start an asynchronous workflow.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Harness supports four lifecycle events:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;table border="1px" width="100%" cellpadding="10px"&gt; 
 &lt;tbody&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Event&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;When it runs&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Effect of a Lambda denial&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;before_invocation&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Before the agent processes the request&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Stops the invocation with &lt;code&gt;hook_stopped&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;before_tool_call&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;After the model proposes a tool call and before execution&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Skips the tool call. The agent loop continues&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;after_tool_call&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;After a tool result exists&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Retains the result and stops before further inference&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;after_invocation&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;After the invocation completes&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Reports the decision. Output already streamed cannot be retracted&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
 &lt;/tbody&gt; 
&lt;/table&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Only Lambda hooks can change the agent loop. They run synchronously and return allow or deny, with an optional reason. SNS and Amazon EventBridge hooks are notifications and don’t block the agent loop.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;For example, a customer-support agent can investigate an order on its own while a &lt;code&gt;before_tool_call&lt;/code&gt; hook checks each refund against an approval limit before the refund tool runs. The following configuration attaches that Lambda function to the harness:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-json"&gt;[
    {
        "beforeToolCall": {
            "name": "refund_policy",
            "target": {
                "lambda": {
                    "arn": "arn:aws:lambda:us-west-2:123456789012:function:refund-policy",
                    "timeoutSeconds": 10,
                    "failureMode": "deny"
                }
            }
        }
    }
]&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The function receives the proposed tool call in the hook payload and returns a decision:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-python"&gt;def lambda_handler(event, _context):
    tool = event["context"]
    if tool["toolName"] == "issue_refund" and tool["toolInput"]["amount"] &amp;gt; 500:
        return {"decision": "deny", "reason": "Refund exceeds the $500 auto-approval limit."}
    return {"decision": "allow"}&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;When the function denies the call, the harness skips the refund and the agent continues, so it can tell the customer that the request was escalated. Each decision also appears in the &lt;code&gt;InvokeHarness&lt;/code&gt; response stream as a &lt;code&gt;hookEvent&lt;/code&gt;:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-json"&gt;{
    "hookEvent": {
        "name": "refund_policy",
        "type": "before_tool_call",
        "decision": "deny",
        "reason": "Refund exceeds the $500 auto-approval limit."
    }
}&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Hooks also apply to subagents. When the agent delegates a task, each tool call the subagent makes runs through the same &lt;code&gt;beforeToolCall&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code&gt;afterToolCall&lt;/code&gt; hooks, so delegation can’t bypass your refund policy. The invocation-level hooks (&lt;code&gt;beforeInvocation&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;afterInvocation&lt;/code&gt;) run once for the whole invocation.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;You configure hooks with &lt;code&gt;CreateHarness&lt;/code&gt; or &lt;code&gt;UpdateHarness&lt;/code&gt;. If a Lambda function times out or returns an error, the hook applies its &lt;code&gt;failureMode&lt;/code&gt;, which defaults to deny. For timeouts, payload fields, and context limits, see Lifecycle hooks in the &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock-agentcore/latest/devguide/harness-lifecycle-hooks.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock AgentCore Developer Guide&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;To see every event and target type working together, try the Refund Desk sample in the Amazon Bedrock AgentCore &lt;a href="https://github.com/awslabs/agentcore-samples/tree/main/01-features/01-harness/02-use-cases/05-refund-desk-hooks" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;samples repository on GitHub&lt;/a&gt;. A six-step guided tour shows all four lifecycle events and the AWS Lambda, Amazon SNS, and Amazon EventBridge targets. It also shows validation of client-supplied inline function results and the difference between &lt;code&gt;failureMode&lt;/code&gt; deny and allow.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="run-a-multi-agent-research-coordinator-with-the-agentcore-cli"&gt;Run a multi-agent research coordinator with the AgentCore CLI&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Creating a harness takes a single command. You name it and set up the instructions, context policy, and limits you want. This example creates a Research Coordinator: a harness with a task-oriented system prompt and summarization-based truncation. In a single invocation, it runs a researcher, a synthesizer, and a reviewer as subagents and returns one consolidated report. You get multi-agent orchestration and context management without provisioning infrastructure or wiring the delegation yourself.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The &lt;code&gt;agentcore harness&lt;/code&gt; commands call the harness APIs directly, so you don’t need a CLI project or a deployment step. Install the AgentCore CLI, a capability of Amazon Bedrock AgentCore, with npm:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-bash"&gt;npm install -g @aws/agentcore&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The CLI uses your default AWS credentials. It has no &lt;code&gt;--profile&lt;/code&gt; flag, so set the &lt;code&gt;AWS_PROFILE&lt;/code&gt; environment variable if you want a specific profile. Save the coordinator’s instructions from the sample as &lt;code&gt;system_prompt.md&lt;/code&gt; in your working directory.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="create-the-harness"&gt;Create the harness&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;This command passes the coordinator’s system prompt, a summarization policy that keeps the 30 most recent messages intact as the conversation grows, and limits high enough for the full workflow. Because it omits the model, memory, and execution role, the harness uses the current AgentCore recommended model and managed memory, and the CLI creates an execution role in IAM for you.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-bash"&gt;HARNESS_ID=$(agentcore harness create --region us-west-2 --json \
--name ResearchCoordinator \
--system-prompt "$(cat system_prompt.md)" \
--truncation '{"strategy":"summarization","config":{"summarization":{"summaryRatio":0.3,"preserveRecentMessages":30}}}' \
--max-iterations 100 \
--timeout-seconds 3600 \
| jq -r '.harness.harnessId')&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Creation is asynchronous. Wait for creation to finish. The last command should print &lt;code&gt;READY&lt;/code&gt;:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-bash"&gt;while [ "$(agentcore harness get --region us-west-2 --id "$HARNESS_ID" --json | jq -r '.harness.status')" = CREATING ]; do sleep 10; done

agentcore harness get --region us-west-2 --id "$HARNESS_ID" --json | jq -r '.harness.status'&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="send-the-prompts"&gt;Send the prompts&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Invoke your harness with a new session ID. Start with a basic question. The coordinator answers it directly, without calling its subagents:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-bash"&gt;SESSION_ID=$(uuidgen)
agentcore harness invoke --region us-west-2 \
--id "$HARNESS_ID" \
--session-id "$SESSION_ID" \
--prompt "What is the current trending topic in AI?"&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Then ask for a full research report in the same session. The coordinator plans the work and delegates to the researcher, synthesizer, and reviewer in order. It runs at most one revision cycle if the reviewer asks for it, and delivers the final report. Reusing the session ID continues the conversation in the same environment.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-bash"&gt;agentcore harness invoke --region us-west-2 \
--id "$HARNESS_ID" \
--session-id "$SESSION_ID" \
--prompt "Perform a full research report on how data shapes innovations in the tech industry. Use specialized subagents to complete this task. In the final output, clearly state which part each subagent was responsible for, followed by their specific findings."&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;With &lt;code&gt;--prompt&lt;/code&gt;, the CLI prints the completed transcript as JSON when the turn finishes. The research run takes about 10 to 15 minutes. To follow along as it works, leave out &lt;code&gt;--prompt&lt;/code&gt;, and the CLI opens an interactive chat.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The same example in Python (boto3), including the full system prompt, is available in the AgentCore samples repository on GitHub.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="clean-up"&gt;Clean up&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Delete the harness when you’re done. This also deletes its managed memory unless you pass &lt;code&gt;--delete-managed-memory false&lt;/code&gt;. The execution role that the CLI created stays in your account, so delete it in the IAM console if you no longer need it.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-bash"&gt;agentcore harness delete --region us-west-2 --id "$HARNESS_ID"&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="rollout-and-compatibility"&gt;Rollout and compatibility&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;On November 11, AgentCore harness began rolling out the Strands harness-backed experience to new and existing harnesses. No customer action is required.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Configuration you explicitly provide remains unchanged. Harnesses that omit the model, system prompt, or &lt;code&gt;truncation&lt;/code&gt; configuration, or use &lt;code&gt;allowedTools: ["*"]&lt;/code&gt;, receive the upgraded managed defaults.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;If your application requires fixed behavior or a fixed capability boundary, configure those settings explicitly.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="learn-more"&gt;Learn more&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Create and invoke an AgentCore harness as you do today. Use the managed defaults for the complete current recommended experience, or configure individual settings when your application requires fixed behavior.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href="https://strandsagents.com/blog/introducing-strands-harness/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Strands harness announcement&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock-agentcore/latest/devguide/harness-get-started.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Get started&lt;/a&gt; with AgentCore harness.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock-agentcore/latest/devguide/harness-models.html#harness-default-instructions" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Managed system prompt&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;AgentCore harness &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock-agentcore-control/latest/APIReference/API_CreateHarness.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;API reference&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock-agentcore/latest/devguide/harness-lifecycle-hooks.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Lifecycle hooks&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Refund Desk lifecycle hooks &lt;a href="https://github.com/awslabs/agentcore-samples/tree/main/01-features/01-harness/02-use-cases" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;code sample&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;We look forward to seeing what you build with the upgraded AgentCore harness.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;hr style="width: 100%"&gt;
