		<item>
		<title>Amazon Polly launches voice cloning: A unique voice for your brand</title>
		<link>https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/amazon-polly-launches-voice-cloning-a-unique-voice-for-your-brand/</link>
		
		<dc:creator><![CDATA[Suyin Wang]]></dc:creator>
		<pubDate>Thu, 19 Nov 2026 19:53:32 +0000</pubDate>
				<category><![CDATA[Amazon Polly]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Announcements]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Intermediate (200)]]></category>
		<guid isPermaLink="false">0284d123338e8b4392bdbafcf50be65e5c2ba081</guid>

					<description>Voice cloning in Amazon Polly is now generally available. Give Amazon Polly a few minutes of recordings from a consenting speaker, and it builds a custom voice that reads new text in that speaker's voice. Cloned voices run on the Amazon Polly generative engine and support seven languages.</description>
										<content:encoded>&lt;p&gt;Voice cloning in Amazon Polly is now generally available as a gated release. Give Amazon Polly a few minutes of audio recordings from a speaker who has agreed to have their voice cloned, and Amazon Polly builds your own cloned voice in that speaker’s voice. The cloned voices run on the Amazon Polly generative engine, which delivers similar naturalness and accuracy as the rest of the generative voices. Amazon Polly voice cloning launches with seven languages: English, Spanish, German, French, Italian, Portuguese, and Hindi. Each cloned voice speaks the language it was recorded in. The cloned voices are automatically encrypted and access-controlled: only you can access your cloned voices.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Amazon Polly voice cloning is available in all the AWS Regions supported by the Amazon Polly generative engine, including the AWS GovCloud (US) Region. Access is already enabled for select Amazon Polly customers. Others can reach out to their AWS account team or &lt;a href="https://pages.awscloud.com/amazon-polly-voice-cloning-20261119.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;apply for access&lt;/a&gt; to get access.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;With this new feature addition, you can build the cloning lifecycle into AWS applications. Building a cloned voice is free for Amazon Polly customers, and Amazon Polly charges for cloned voice usage on a pay-as-you-go basis, see &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/polly/pricing/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Polly Pricing page&lt;/a&gt; for details.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="hear-it-for-yourself"&gt;Hear it for yourself&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Press play: the first few seconds are a real recording of the speaker, and then their cloned voice takes over with words they never recorded.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;From recordings to TTS voice.&lt;/strong&gt; A few minutes of our science lead Trevor’s source audio, and the resulting generative voice. 
  &lt;ul&gt; 
   &lt;li&gt;&lt;em&gt;Recorded:&lt;/em&gt; “Hello, I’m Trevor, from Science.” &lt;em&gt;Cloned voice:&lt;/em&gt; “That first sentence was my real voice. This is my cloned voice, created from a few minutes of my recordings. It can read any text you give it.”
    &lt;audio class="wp-audio-shortcode" id="audio-145672-1" preload="none" style="width: 100%;" controls="controls"&gt;
     &lt;source type="audio/mpeg" src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/artifacts/DBSBlogs/ML-22432/1-hero-trevor.mp3?_=1"&gt;
    &lt;/audio&gt;&lt;/li&gt; 
  &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Supports seven languages.&lt;/strong&gt; Hear Jonas, one of the authors, speaking German in his recording and then in his cloned voice: 
  &lt;ul&gt; 
   &lt;li&gt;Recorded: “Hallo, ich bin Jonas. Das ist meine echte Stimme.” (Hi, I’m Jonas. This is my real voice.) &lt;em&gt;Cloned voice:&lt;/em&gt; “Und das ist meine geklonte Stimme! Gleiche Stimme, neue Worte: Diesen Satz habe ich geschrieben, aber nie aufgenommen.” (And this is my cloned voice! Same voice, new words: I wrote this sentence, but never recorded it.)
    &lt;audio class="wp-audio-shortcode" id="audio-145672-2" preload="none" style="width: 100%;" controls="controls"&gt;
     &lt;source type="audio/mpeg" src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/artifacts/DBSBlogs/ML-22432/2-de-switch-jonas.mp3?_=2"&gt;
    &lt;/audio&gt;&lt;/li&gt; 
  &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Use in a variety of applications.&lt;/strong&gt; Here’s Trevor in a new job he hasn’t had: a non-player character in a game. 
  &lt;ul&gt; 
   &lt;li&gt;“Who’s that crossing my bridge? Oh, it’s you again. Same deal as last time: answer my riddle, or hand over the sandwich.”
    &lt;audio class="wp-audio-shortcode" id="audio-145672-3" preload="none" style="width: 100%;" controls="controls"&gt;

  &lt;ul&gt; 
   &lt;li&gt;&lt;em&gt;Recorded:&lt;/em&gt; “Hi, I’m Jeff, and this is how I really sound.” &lt;em&gt;Cloned voice:&lt;/em&gt; “And this is how my cloned voice sounds, reading a sentence I never recorded.”

&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="what-you-can-build"&gt;What you can build&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;A consistent brand voice across channels&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Establish one recognizable voice and use it across your interactive voice response (IVR) system, cascaded voice agents, mobile app, in-store announcements, advertising, and connected devices. Rather than relying on a generic stock voice or re-booking a recording session each time a script changes, you can generate on-brand audio on demand, with a consistent sound in every channel.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Contact centers&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Deliver a distinctive, on-brand voice across self-service flows, and update prompts as offers and policies change without a new studio session. You can create prompts from source text, test them before rollout, and use the same voice across greeting, routing, and notification experiences.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Media and content at scale&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Creators, publishers, and studios can create audiobooks, courses, podcasts, game characters, and videos in multiple custom voices to enhance the listening experience, and produce subsequent chapters or updates quickly.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Localization with a consistent persona&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Maintain a recognizable voice identity across a content library, so narration remains coherent across titles and updates. Each cloned voice speaks only the language it was recorded in. For multilingual experiences, create and review approved voices per supported language.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Accessibility and personalization&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Offer a natural, human-sounding voice for reading experiences, assistive tools, and personalized interactions. You confirm the speaker’s consent each time you create a voice.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="how-voice-cloning-works"&gt;How voice cloning works&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;With voice cloning, you can build a custom Amazon Polly voice from recordings of a single speaker and then synthesize speech in that voice through the Amazon Polly generative engine. The voice moves through four steps, from recording the speaker to using the finished voice:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/19/ML-22432-1.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/19/ML-22432-1.png" alt="The voice cloning lifecycle: record, create, wait, use" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 1: The voice cloning lifecycle, from recording a speaker to using the finished voice&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;A cloned voice goes through four steps:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ol type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Record.&lt;/strong&gt; You record one speaker (see the following recording tips). You can send the recordings directly in the &lt;code&gt;CreateVoice&lt;/code&gt; request or upload them on the console. You can also store them in an Amazon Simple Storage Service (Amazon S3) bucket in the AWS Region where you want the voice and point Amazon Polly to them.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Create.&lt;/strong&gt; You call &lt;code&gt;CreateVoice&lt;/code&gt; with 1-10 recordings, the language they are spoken in, and &lt;code&gt;TermsAccepted=True&lt;/code&gt;, your confirmation that you have the speaker’s consent. Amazon Polly returns a voice ID (&lt;code&gt;vc-…&lt;/code&gt;) right away and builds the voice in the background.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Wait.&lt;/strong&gt; Creation is asynchronous and typically takes 5-10 minutes. During that time, Amazon Polly also checks the quality of your text-to-speech (TTS) voice for speaker similarity. You call &lt;code&gt;GetVoice&lt;/code&gt; until the status changes from &lt;code&gt;pending&lt;/code&gt; to &lt;code&gt;active&lt;/code&gt; or &lt;code&gt;failed&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Use.&lt;/strong&gt; You pass the voice ID to &lt;code&gt;SynthesizeSpeech&lt;/code&gt; for real-time audio or to &lt;code&gt;StartSpeechSynthesisTask&lt;/code&gt; for long-form audio written to S3, with &lt;code&gt;Engine="generative"&lt;/code&gt;, the same way you call the other generative voices in Amazon Polly. When you no longer need the voice, you call &lt;code&gt;DeleteVoice&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="consent-and-control"&gt;Consent and control&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;A cloned voice is made from one person’s recordings. You confirm that you have the speaker’s consent for all recordings and that you control who can use the voice and when it’s deleted.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;You confirm consent for each voice you create. When you create a voice, on the console or with &lt;code&gt;TermsAccepted=True&lt;/code&gt; in the API, you confirm that you have obtained all rights and consents necessary to create and use the cloned voice. Your use of voice cloning is subject to the &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/service-terms/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Service Terms&lt;/a&gt;, the &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/legal/biometric-notice-and-consent-terms" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Biometric Notice and Consent Terms&lt;/a&gt;, the &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/ai/responsible-ai/policy/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Responsible AI Policy&lt;/a&gt;, and the &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/aup/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Acceptable Use Policy&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Generated speech carries a watermark. Amazon Polly adds a watermark to text-to-speech audio generated with the cloned voice.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Only your account can use it. A cloned voice is scoped to the AWS account that created it, and you control access with AWS Identity and Access Management (IAM). You can encrypt voices with your own AWS Key Management Service (AWS KMS) key and tag them.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;You decide when it goes away. Delete a voice with one &lt;code&gt;DeleteVoice&lt;/code&gt; call or on the console, once it’s active or its creation has failed.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Amazon Polly does not retain a copy of your voice recordings once the voice is created and doesn’t use them to train Amazon Polly models. It keeps only the voice data it needs to synthesize speech, and stores it encrypted. You can use your own AWS KMS key for that encryption, and &lt;code&gt;DeleteVoice&lt;/code&gt; removes the data immediately.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;We recommend that you:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Keep a record of each speaker’s consent.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Delete the voice if the speaker withdraws consent.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Tell listeners when they’re hearing a synthetic voice where that’s appropriate.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Grant voice permissions only to the people and systems that need them.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;For details on intended uses and limitations, see the &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/ai/responsible-ai/amazon-polly/overview.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Polly AI Service Card&lt;/a&gt; and the &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/ai/responsible-ai/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Responsible Use of AI&lt;/a&gt; guidance.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="best-practices-for-recordings"&gt;Best practices for recordings&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Your cloned voice learns from the recording, including the parts you’d rather it didn’t: the laptop fan, the room echo, the meeting app’s noise filter.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Record two to ten minutes of natural speech, split into takes of up to a minute. You can send up to 10 recordings per voice, and up to 15 minutes of audio in total. Quality matters more than length: more audio might not change how the voice sounds.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;One speaker, a quiet room, the same distance from the mic. Use a USB microphone, or a phone app that can record WAV. Record in mono WAV, 16-bit, at 24 kHz from the start, so there’s nothing to convert later. Amazon Polly accepts other audio formats, but we recommend WAV. See the &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/polly/latest/dg/what-is.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Polly Developer Guide&lt;/a&gt; for supported formats and limits.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Avoid using meeting apps and browser recorders. Their echo cancellation and noise suppression change the voice, and the clone learns those changes too.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Record every take in the style you want the voice to have. The cloned voice takes its style from the recordings and can’t blend several, so upbeat, sad, and angry takes won’t give you a voice that can do all three.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Record in the language the voice should speak. Voice cloning is tested with recordings in US English, German, US Spanish, French, Italian, Brazilian Portuguese, and Hindi. Recordings in other accents of these languages, such as British English or Canadian French, also work but are less tested, so check the voice with your own content.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="getting-started"&gt;Getting started&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;On the console:&lt;/strong&gt; once voice cloning is enabled for your account (see Availability), expand the left panel and choose &lt;strong&gt;Voice cloning&lt;/strong&gt;, upload up to 10 recordings or select them from Amazon S3, confirm the terms, and choose &lt;strong&gt;Create&lt;/strong&gt;. Each upload can be up to 5 MB. When the voice is ready, try it out on its details page.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;In code&lt;/strong&gt;, you need:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;The latest version of boto3 (&lt;code&gt;pip install --upgrade boto3&lt;/code&gt;).&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;IAM permissions for &lt;code&gt;polly:CreateVoice&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;polly:GetVoice&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;polly:DescribeVoices&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;polly:SynthesizeSpeech&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code&gt;polly:DeleteVoice&lt;/code&gt;. For long-form audio, also &lt;code&gt;polly:StartSpeechSynthesisTask&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;polly:GetSpeechSynthesisTask&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code&gt;s3:PutObject&lt;/code&gt; on the output bucket.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;For the following Amazon S3 example: your recordings in a bucket in the Region where you create the voice, with &lt;code&gt;s3:GetObject&lt;/code&gt; permission for the IAM role or user that calls &lt;code&gt;CreateVoice&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-python"&gt;import time

import boto3

# Use the Region of your S3 recordings.
polly = boto3.client("polly", region_name="us-east-1")

# 1. Create a voice from recordings of a speaker who has given consent.
#    Use 1-10 clean WAV takes of up to a minute each.
takes = [
    "s3://amzn-s3-demo-bucket/brand-voice/take-1.wav",
    "s3://amzn-s3-demo-bucket/brand-voice/take-2.wav",
    "s3://amzn-s3-demo-bucket/brand-voice/take-3.wav",
]
voice = polly.create_voice(
    Name="MyBrandVoice",    # letters, digits, - and _, up to 64 characters
    LanguageCode="en-US",   # the language spoken in the recordings
    TermsAccepted=True,     # you confirm the speaker's consent and the terms of use
    VoiceCreationData={
        "Recordings": [{"Format": "wav", "Data": {"S3Url": url}} for url in takes],
    },
    Tags={"consent-record": "speaker-0042"},  # optional: the ID of your own consent record, e.g. a signed release
    # Optional: encrypt the voice with your own KMS key (symmetric, same Region).
    # Callers who create or use the voice need kms:DescribeKey,
    # kms:GenerateDataKey and kms:Decrypt on the key.
    # CustomerManagedEncryption={
    #     "KmsKeyArn": "arn:aws:kms:us-east-1:111122223333:key/EXAMPLE"
    # },
)["Voice"]
voice_id = voice["Id"]      # for example "vc-0123456789"

# 2. Check the status every 30 seconds until the voice is active or failed
#    (usually a few minutes). Stop with an error after 20 minutes.
deadline = time.monotonic() + 20 * 60
while voice["Status"] not in ("active", "failed"):
    if time.monotonic() &amp;gt; deadline:
        raise TimeoutError(f"{voice_id} is still {voice['Status']}")
    time.sleep(30)
    voice = polly.get_voice(VoiceId=voice_id)["Voice"]
if voice["Status"] == "failed":
    # Check the recordings, delete this voice and create a new one.
    raise RuntimeError(f"{voice_id}: {voice.get('StatusReason', 'creation failed')}")

# 3. Synthesize speech with your new voice.
speech = polly.synthesize_speech(
    VoiceId=voice_id,
    Engine="generative",
    LanguageCode="en-US",
    OutputFormat="mp3",
    Text="Hello! This is my very own Amazon Polly voice.",
)
with open("my-voice.mp3", "wb") as f:
    f.write(speech["AudioStream"].read())

# When you no longer need the voice:
# polly.delete_voice(VoiceId=voice_id)&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;If Python reports that the &lt;code&gt;Polly&lt;/code&gt; object has no attribute &lt;code&gt;create_voice&lt;/code&gt;, upgrade boto3.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;For long texts such as audiobook chapters or course modules, use an asynchronous synthesis task that writes the audio to S3:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-python"&gt;from pathlib import Path

task = polly.start_speech_synthesis_task(
    VoiceId=voice_id,
    Engine="generative",
    LanguageCode="en-US",
    OutputFormat="mp3",
    OutputS3BucketName="amzn-s3-demo-bucket",
    OutputS3KeyPrefix="narration/chapter-01",
    Text=Path("chapter-01.txt").read_text(encoding="utf-8"),
    # SnsTopicArn="arn:aws:sns:us-east-1:111122223333:polly-tasks",  # notify instead of polling
)["SynthesisTask"]
print(task["TaskId"], task["OutputUri"])  # poll get_speech_synthesis_task until "completed" or "failed"&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;For additional end-to-end Speech AI applications you can build with Amazon Polly, including the voice cloning feature, such as cascaded voice agents and live speech translators, go to the &lt;a href="https://github.com/aws-samples/amazon-ai-samples/tree/main/text-to-speech" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Polly GitHub repo&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="production-considerations"&gt;Production considerations&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Create voices ahead of time&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Voice creation takes minutes, so treat it as a provisioning step, not part of a user request. A small workflow (for example, AWS Step Functions or a job queue) can call &lt;code&gt;CreateVoice&lt;/code&gt;, check &lt;code&gt;GetVoice&lt;/code&gt; every 30 seconds until the voice is active, and then store the voice ID in your configuration. &lt;code&gt;CreateVoice&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code&gt;DeleteVoice&lt;/code&gt; share a limit of 1 request per second, so queue bulk creations and deletions.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Keep an inventory&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;code&gt;DescribeVoices&lt;/code&gt; lists your cloned voices alongside the voices built into Amazon Polly. Cloned voices have &lt;code&gt;Origin&lt;/code&gt; set to &lt;code&gt;customer&lt;/code&gt;. Store the voice ID, Region, language, consent reference, and owner for each voice, and give each voice a unique &lt;code&gt;Name&lt;/code&gt;. If a &lt;code&gt;CreateVoice&lt;/code&gt; call times out or returns a server error, the voice might already exist, and the SDK’s automatic retries can create a second one. Check &lt;code&gt;DescribeVoices&lt;/code&gt; for that name before you try again.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Plan for errors&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;code&gt;CreateVoice&lt;/code&gt; returns &lt;code&gt;ThrottlingException&lt;/code&gt; when you exceed the 1-request-per-second limit it shares with &lt;code&gt;DeleteVoice&lt;/code&gt;, and &lt;code&gt;ServiceQuotaExceededException&lt;/code&gt; when the account has reached its cloned-voice quota in that Region. Synthesizing with, or deleting, a voice that is still being created returns &lt;code&gt;ConflictException&lt;/code&gt;. If a voice uses your own KMS key, callers without access to the key get an access-denied error. When creation fails, &lt;code&gt;StatusReason&lt;/code&gt; gives only a general message, so check the recordings against the preceding recording best practices, then delete the failed voice and create a new one.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Scope permissions to voices&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Cloned voices are IAM resources, so you can give the role that serves traffic permission to synthesize with one voice of choice. If the voice uses your own KMS key, that role also needs access to the key. Keep &lt;code&gt;polly:CreateVoice&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code&gt;polly:DeleteVoice&lt;/code&gt; for a separate provisioning role. Here’s an example policy for the serving role:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-json"&gt;{
  "Version": "2012-10-17",
  "Statement": [
    {
      "Effect": "Allow",
      "Action": "polly:SynthesizeSpeech",
      "Resource": "arn:aws:polly:us-east-1:111122223333:voice/vc-0123456789"
    }
  ]
}&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;With tags, you can write the same rule once for a whole group of voices using &lt;code&gt;aws:ResourceTag&lt;/code&gt; conditions. Review your existing policies too: a statement that allows &lt;code&gt;polly:SynthesizeSpeech&lt;/code&gt; on all resources (&lt;code&gt;"Resource": "*"&lt;/code&gt;) also covers every cloned voice in the account.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Plan for quotas and Regions&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Each account has an initial quota of 10 cloned voices per Region. To request an increase, contact your account team or &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/contact-us/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Support&lt;/a&gt;. A voice exists only in the account and Region where you created it. To serve another Region or account, create the voice again in that Region from the same recordings, and make sure the speaker’s consent covers every copy.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Test with your real scripts&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Before a voice goes live, listen to it reading your own content: product names, numbers, addresses, and the longest sentences you have. The same text can come out slightly differently from one request to the next. For fixed prompts such as phone-line greetings, generate the audio once, approve it with the speaker and your brand team, and serve the stored file. If a name or term comes out wrong, spell it differently in the text or use a Speech Synthesis Markup Language (SSML) &lt;code&gt;&amp;lt;phoneme&amp;gt;&lt;/code&gt; tag. Phoneme tags work with cloned voices in English, French, Italian, German, and Spanish. See &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/polly/latest/dg/supportedtags.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Supported SSML tags&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="things-to-know"&gt;Things to know&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;table border="1px" width="100%" cellpadding="10px"&gt; 
 &lt;tbody&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Availability&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Access is enabled per AWS account. If you see Voice cloning in the Amazon Polly console (expand the left panel and see if Voice cloning is listed), it’s enabled for your account. If not, you can reach out to your AWS account team or &lt;a href="https://pages.awscloud.com/amazon-polly-voice-cloning-20261119.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;apply for access&lt;/a&gt; to request access. Supported AWS Regions are the same as the generative engine: US East (N. Virginia), US West (Oregon), Europe (Frankfurt), Europe (Zurich), Europe (London), Asia Pacific (Sydney), Asia Pacific (Seoul), Asia Pacific (Singapore), Asia Pacific (Tokyo), Canada (Central), and AWS GovCloud (US-West). A cloned voice lives in the AWS Region where you created it.&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Languages&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;English, German, Spanish, French, Italian, Portuguese, and Hindi. Tested recording locales: US English (&lt;code&gt;en-US&lt;/code&gt;), German (&lt;code&gt;de-DE&lt;/code&gt;), US Spanish (&lt;code&gt;es-US&lt;/code&gt;), French (&lt;code&gt;fr-FR&lt;/code&gt;), Italian (&lt;code&gt;it-IT&lt;/code&gt;), Brazilian Portuguese (&lt;code&gt;pt-BR&lt;/code&gt;), and Hindi (&lt;code&gt;hi-IN&lt;/code&gt;). Other accents of these languages also work but are less tested. Each voice speaks the language it was recorded in.&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Recordings&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;1-10 recordings per voice, up to 15 minutes in total. WAV is recommended: mono, 16-bit, at 24 kHz or 44.1/48 kHz. Amazon Polly accepts other audio formats too. Console uploads are limited to 5 MB per file. For larger files, use Amazon S3.&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Data handling&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Amazon Polly deletes its copy of your recordings once the voice is created and doesn’t use them to train Amazon Polly models. It keeps only the voice data needed for synthesis, stored encrypted (you can use your own AWS KMS key), until you call &lt;code&gt;DeleteVoice&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Creation time&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Typically takes 5-10 minutes to build a TTS voice, asynchronous.&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Synthesis latency&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Same as Generative voices in Amazon Polly.&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Quotas&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;10 cloned voices per account per AWS Region initially. To request an increase, contact AWS Support. &lt;code&gt;CreateVoice&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code&gt;DeleteVoice&lt;/code&gt; share a limit of 1 request per second.&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Synthesis&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Cloned voices follow the Amazon Polly quotas for generative voices, including the text limits per request and per synthesis task. See &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/polly/latest/dg/limits.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Polly quotas&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Pricing&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Creating a cloned voice is free. Amazon Polly charges for cloned voice usage on a pay-as-you-go basis. See &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/polly/pricing/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Polly pricing&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;APIs&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;CreateVoice&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;GetVoice&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;DeleteVoice&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code&gt;DescribeVoices&lt;/code&gt; to manage voices. &lt;code&gt;SynthesizeSpeech&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code&gt;StartSpeechSynthesisTask&lt;/code&gt; with &lt;code&gt;Engine="generative"&lt;/code&gt; to use them. See the &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/polly/latest/dg/API_Reference.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Polly API reference&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
 &lt;/tbody&gt; 
&lt;/table&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="conclusion"&gt;Conclusion&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Voice cloning in Amazon Polly gives your application a voice people recognize: recorded once, with the speaker’s consent, and ready to use through the Amazon Polly APIs. To get started on the console, make sure Voice cloning is enabled for your account, prepare a recording and the required consent, open the &lt;a href="https://console.aws.amazon.com/polly/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Polly console&lt;/a&gt;, and create your first cloned voice. If you are a new customer and would like to enable voice cloning, reach out to your AWS account team or &lt;a href="https://pages.awscloud.com/amazon-polly-voice-cloning-20261119.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;apply for access&lt;/a&gt; to get access.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;For the developer guide, refer to the &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/polly/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Polly documentation&lt;/a&gt;. For pricing, see the &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/polly/pricing/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Polly Pricing Page&lt;/a&gt;. For additional speech AI use cases you can build with Amazon Polly, including voice cloning, check out the &lt;a href="https://github.com/aws-samples/amazon-ai-samples/tree/main/text-to-speech" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;GitHub repo&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;We can’t wait to hear what you build.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;em&gt;Thanks to Trevor Wood, Jonas Rohnke, Suyin Wang, Hannah Barrett, Nikie Xiong, Jeff Barr, and Joely Huang for lending us their voices.&lt;/em&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;hr style="width: 100%"&gt;

		<item>
		<title>Optimize AI economics on Amazon Bedrock with Accenture’s sequenced approach</title>
		<link>https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/optimize-ai-economics-on-amazon-bedrock-with-accentures-sequenced-approach/</link>
		
		<dc:creator><![CDATA[Ilan Geller]]></dc:creator>
		<pubDate>Thu, 19 Nov 2026 18:48:55 +0000</pubDate>
				<category><![CDATA[Amazon Bedrock]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Customer Solutions]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Expert (400)]]></category>
		<guid isPermaLink="false">956ccf301033a9414ae29d032c3df0b75681b861</guid>

					<description>As enterprises scale AI on Amazon Bedrock, token spend can grow even as per-token prices fall. This post walks through six composable optimization levers, shows how Accenture applied them across four client engagements, and recommends a sequence for cutting AI token spend on your own workloads.</description>
										<content:encoded>&lt;p&gt;As enterprises scale AI on &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock&lt;/a&gt;, token spend often grows even as per-token prices fall. Without cost allocation tags in place, your cloud invoice shows AI spend as a single line item rather than by team, workload, agent, or model. Cheaper tokens expand adoption, and agentic workloads multiply invocations. As successful AI use cases scale, consumption can rise faster than unit prices drop. The economic question is whether the business value generated grows faster than the cost of the intelligence required to produce it. The Linux Foundation has launched the &lt;a href="https://www.linuxfoundation.org/press/linux-foundation-launches-the-tokenomics-foundation-to-define-the-economics-and-roi-of-ai-value" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Tokenomics Foundation&lt;/a&gt; to develop open standards for AI cost economics, a sign that managing the economics of AI is now an industry discipline.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;In this post, you will learn about six levers for optimizing token spend, how Accenture applied them in production, and how to apply them using Amazon Bedrock.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Accenture applied these levers in four client engagements across telecom, insurance, mining, and a large enterprise deployment. The same levers are available natively on Amazon Bedrock as described in this post. Where savings were measured, token spend fell by about 40–85 percent. Results are specific to each client’s workload and may vary. Client names are withheld by Accenture to protect client confidentiality.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="attribution-first-measuring-cost-at-the-business-level"&gt;Attribution first: Measuring cost at the business level&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Before you optimize anything, you need to see where consumption is occurring, who owns it, and what business value it produces. Cost allocation tags at the team, workload, and agent level are the prerequisite for connecting consumption to accountable owners and, ultimately, to business outcomes. You apply them through &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/cost-mgmt-application-inference-profiles.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock application inference profiles&lt;/a&gt;, one profile per application or workload, tagged by team, cost center, and environment. After you activate them as cost allocation tags in the &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/aws-cost-management/billing-and-cost-management-console-home/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Billing and Cost Management console&lt;/a&gt;, you can use these tags to trace which teams, applications, and agents are driving spend. They also make it possible to measure the impact of each change you introduce.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Three AWS services provide the underlying telemetry. &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/aws-cost-management/aws-cost-and-usage-reporting/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Cost and Usage Reports&lt;/a&gt; (CUR 2.0), delivered through &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/cur/latest/userguide/what-is-data-exports.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Data Exports&lt;/a&gt;, handle financial attribution, breaking down billed inference cost by workload and team after your cost allocation tags are active. &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/cloudwatch/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon CloudWatch&lt;/a&gt; captures operational signals such as invocation counts and latency. &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/model-invocation-logging.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock model invocation logging&lt;/a&gt; records request-level detail, including the model used and the input and output token counts for each call.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Telemetry alone isn’t enough. Engineers can read cost per invocation on a dashboard, but product owners and program leaders need a metric expressed in business terms. Accenture’s Cost per Business Transaction (CpBT) framework bridges this gap. CpBT aggregates all the model invocations, retries, tool calls, and agent handoffs that contribute to one successful business output into a single fully loaded cost figure. It surfaces the true cost of producing a result, including the failed attempts that raw token pricing hides. Tracked against the business value each workflow delivers, CpBT distinguishes a growing bill that reflects scaling success from one that reflects waste.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="the-six-optimization-levers"&gt;The six optimization levers&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The goal of these six levers isn’t to minimize tokens at all costs. It’s to use the right amount and level of intelligence to deliver each business outcome at the appropriate cost, quality, and risk. The levers are organized into three phases based on the complexity of the change and the investment it requires. AWS and Accenture recommend working through the phases in order, and you can apply each phase independently to the workloads where it will have the most impact.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="phase-1-configuration-only-no-infrastructure-change"&gt;Phase 1: Configuration only, no infrastructure change&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;With &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/prompt-caching.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock prompt caching&lt;/a&gt;, the model reuses the parts of a prompt that repeat across requests, such as system instructions, tool definitions, and reference documents, so you stop paying full price to process the same content again. Amazon Bedrock stores the key-value state of previously computed prompt prefixes and is designed to skip recomputation on later requests. This is designed to reduce cached input token costs by up to 90 percent for supported models (see &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/prompt-caching/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock Prompt Caching&lt;/a&gt;).&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/batch-inference.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock batch inference&lt;/a&gt; processes non-urgent workloads asynchronously as a group at 50 percent lower cost than on-demand pricing for select models (see &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/pricing/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock Pricing&lt;/a&gt;). You submit inference requests as a batch job, and results are written to &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/s3/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Simple Storage Service (Amazon S3)&lt;/a&gt; when processing completes. Batch inference suits nightly reports, document processing pipelines, and evaluation runs that don’t need an immediate response.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Both levers add configuration to your existing setup. Prompt caching requires cache checkpoint markers in your prompts, and batch inference requires submitting eligible requests as a batch job instead of calling the on-demand API. Apply caching to interactive, on-demand traffic and batch inference to asynchronous work. Neither requires new infrastructure or architecture changes.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="phase-2-architectural-changes"&gt;Phase 2: Architectural changes&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/prompt-routing.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock intelligent prompt routing&lt;/a&gt; directs requests across models within the same family, matching each task to the level of intelligence its business value, complexity, and risk require, rather than defaulting every request to the most powerful model. Intelligent prompt routing can reduce costs by up to 30 percent without compromising accuracy (&lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/prompt-routing.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;see Amazon Bedrock intelligent prompt routing&lt;/a&gt;). Check &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/pricing/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock Pricing&lt;/a&gt; for any routing charges. In the following engagements, Accenture’s model tiering and triage routing apply this same routing lever.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/what-is/retrieval-augmented-generation/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Retrieval Augmented Generation&lt;/a&gt; (RAG) optimization tunes the retrieval step in the RAG process so that fewer unnecessary context tokens reach the model. Techniques such as hierarchical chunking, metadata-based filtering, and reranking narrow the retrieved context to the most relevant chunks, so you pay primarily for content that improves the answer.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Two complementary Accenture patterns work alongside these AWS native levers. Context scoping limits the data each agent receives to the document slice relevant to its step of the workflow, rather than passing the full case context at every handoff. Context compression summarizes or filters documents before they reach the model. Both reduce input token volume, especially across multi-agent chains.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="phase-3-infrastructure-and-training-investment"&gt;Phase 3: Infrastructure and training investment&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Semantic caching stores responses and reuses them when a new request is semantically similar to one already answered, even if the wording differs. This avoids redundant model invocations and reduces both cost and latency. On AWS, you can implement it with a vector store such as &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/elasticache/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon ElastiCache&lt;/a&gt; in front of the model. In an AWS benchmark on 63,796 real chatbot queries, a 0.90 similarity threshold produced a 74.5 percent cache hit rate, 92.3 percent accuracy, and 72.5 percent cost savings (&lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonElastiCache/latest/dg/semantic-caching-benchmarks.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;see ElastiCache semantic caching benchmarks&lt;/a&gt;). As you apply routing and semantic caching, keep &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/guardrails/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock Guardrails&lt;/a&gt; on every route, and set semantic cache expiry so that users don’t receive outdated answers.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/model-distillation/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Model distillation&lt;/a&gt; transfers knowledge from a large teacher model to a smaller, faster student model. Distilled models in Amazon Bedrock are &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/model-distillation/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;up to 500 percent faster and up to 75 percent less expensive&lt;/a&gt; than original models, with less than 2 percent accuracy loss for use cases such as RAG. Distillation is best suited to stable, high-volume workloads where the transaction volume justifies the training investment.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="in-production-four-client-engagements"&gt;In production: Four client engagements&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Accenture applied these levers in four client engagements. Each engagement targeted a different cost pattern. In two of them, Accenture applied several changes together, so those results reflect the combined effect rather than any single lever.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The first engagement was a large enterprise deployment where every request went to a frontier model, with no caching, model tiering, or batching. Accenture applied three changes together: model tiering to match each task to the smallest model that could handle it well, prompt caching for repeated context, and batch processing for overnight and non-urgent jobs. Together they cut monthly token cost by 85 percent, and the saving was not separated by change. About 15 percent of tasks still needed the top model for hard, open-ended reasoning, and the rest ran on lighter models. Your split will depend on your workload composition.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The second engagement was a telecom operator with $12 million in annual token spend. Most of that spend came from agents fanning out requests without limits and from every request going to a frontier model regardless of complexity. Accenture applied triage routing, semantic caching, and context compression together. Input tokens fell by 70 percent, and annual token spend fell from $12 million to $3.8 million, a 68 percent reduction. Accenture validated output quality against the operator’s pre-change performance benchmarks.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The third and fourth engagements each applied a single cache layer. An insurer used model-layer prefix caching, reusing the static portions of its prompts across invocations. A mining operator used an application-layer semantic cache, returning stored responses for repeated queries before they reached the model. Across the two engagements, token spend fell by about 40 percent combined, without model routing changes or training investment.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="results"&gt;Results&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Outcomes ranged from about 40 percent to about 85 percent, reflecting each client’s starting point. The large enterprise deployment began with no optimization in place and reached 85 percent. The telecom operator reached 68 percent on $12 million in annual token spend, and the insurer and mining operator reached about 40 percent combined with one caching lever each. Results are specific to each client’s workload and may vary.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;These savings aren’t the end goal. They create capacity to scale AI workloads, reinvest in higher-value use cases, and improve the return generated by each dollar of AI consumption. Two of these engagements applied several changes at once. When you apply one lever at a time, you can attribute each result to a specific change. AWS and Accenture recommend this sequence for your own workloads: connect consumption to owners and outcomes, apply one lever at a time, measure cost, quality, and business value, and build on what works.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="production-governance-four-operating-tenets"&gt;Production governance: Four operating tenets&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Optimizing AI economics in production requires an ongoing governance cycle spanning cost, quality, risk, and business value, not a one-time technical change. Before you apply any lever, establish a quantitative baseline for both cost and quality so that you can measure the impact of each change against a known starting point. When you validate output quality, use held-out evaluation sets drawn from your actual production traffic rather than synthetic benchmarks. Apply one lever at a time per workload so that you can attribute results to a specific change and roll back cleanly if quality drops below your acceptable thresholds. If you operate in a regulated industry such as financial services, insurance, or healthcare, document your quality test results and rollback procedures as part of your standard change management record.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;These four tenets apply regardless of which levers you are using or what phase of the sequencing you are in. &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/agentcore/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock AgentCore&lt;/a&gt; is an environment where you can build, connect, and optimize agents at scale with any framework or model. It includes &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock-agentcore/latest/devguide/evaluations.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AgentCore Evaluations&lt;/a&gt;, a capability of Amazon Bedrock AgentCore that provides batch evaluation to support your quality validation cycle as you progress through more advanced levers.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="getting-started"&gt;Getting started&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;You can begin with three steps. First, turn on cost attribution for Amazon Bedrock by applying cost allocation tags at the team and workload level so that your spend is traceable from day one. Second, activate those tags in the AWS Billing and Cost Management console. Then use CUR 2.0 and model invocation logging to identify high-consumption workloads, and assess each one based on spend, business value, quality requirements, risk, volume, and optimization potential. Third, apply Amazon Bedrock prompt caching to that workload’s interactive traffic and Amazon Bedrock batch inference to its asynchronous jobs as configuration-only changes, and measure the result before you move to architectural changes in Phase 2.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/wellarchitected/latest/agentic-ai-lens/agentic-ai-lens.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Well-Architected Agentic AI Lens&lt;/a&gt; includes &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/wellarchitected/latest/agentic-ai-lens/agentcost02.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;model invocation and token cost optimization&lt;/a&gt; (AGENTCOST02), a focus area within the cost optimization pillar for agentic workloads. This focus area provides a structured framework you can use to apply these levers at scale across your organization.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;hr style="width: 100%"&gt;

		<item>
		<title>From learning to leaderboard: How Cisco upskilled 1,200 engineers in agentic AI with AWS AI League</title>
		<link>https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/from-learning-to-leaderboard-how-cisco-upskilled-1200-engineers-in-agentic-ai-with-aws-ai-league/</link>
		
		<dc:creator><![CDATA[Rakesh Vangari]]></dc:creator>
		<pubDate>Thu, 19 Nov 2026 18:26:22 +0000</pubDate>
				<category><![CDATA[Amazon Bedrock AgentCore]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Amazon SageMaker AI]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Customer Solutions]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Intermediate (200)]]></category>
		<guid isPermaLink="false">f3760d486fc3a253f618f5aadd62c89dc2633b7d</guid>

					<description>Cisco and AWS used Cisco Transformation Fest and AWS AI League to turn hands-on AI learning into a culture of building. A Cisco engineer won the 2025 AWS AI League global title and 1,200 engineers competed in 2026. This post shares Cisco's journey, lessons from the 2026 winning agent built on Amazon Bedrock AgentCore, and how to host your own league.</description>
										<content:encoded>&lt;p&gt;Cisco, like many large engineering organizations, is adopting generative and agentic AI to change how its engineers build software. The hard part is no longer access to the technology. It’s helping thousands of engineers move from watching a demo to confidently building with it. Recorded sessions and slide decks rarely close that gap. Engineers learn fastest when they build something real, see it fail, fix it, and try again.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;AWS designed &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/ai/aileague/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS AI League&lt;/a&gt; for exactly that. It turns hands-on AI learning into a gamified competition, where participants customize models and build agents on AWS, get instant feedback on a live leaderboard, and compete in a game-show-style finale.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Cisco is one of the clearest examples of what this looks like at scale. In 2025, Hemanth V, a Cisco software engineer, went from an internal Cisco event to being crowned the first AWS AI League Champion at AWS re:Invent 2025. In 2026, 1,200 Cisco engineers competed in an AWS AI League tournament at Cisco Transformation Fest 4.0. The winner, Nishanth Aluhonnu, earned a full event pass to AWS re:Invent 2026 in Las Vegas.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;In this post, we share how Cisco and AWS used Cisco Transformation Fest and AWS AI League to build a lasting culture of learning by doing. We previously covered this program in &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/from-theory-to-delivery-how-atos-upskilled-400-engineers-in-agentic-ai/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;From theory to delivery: How Atos upskilled 400 engineers in agentic AI&lt;/a&gt;, and here we look at Cisco’s experience. We walk through Cisco’s journey, share technical lessons from the 2026 winning agent, and explain how your organization can host its own AWS AI League.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h1&gt;What is AWS AI League?&lt;/h1&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;AWS AI League helps organizations build advanced AI skills through competition. Each league follows three steps:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ol type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Learn –&lt;/strong&gt;&amp;nbsp;A hands-on, 2-hour workshop led by AWS experts introduces the tools and the challenge.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Build –&lt;/strong&gt;&amp;nbsp;Participants experiment at their own pace and submit to a live leaderboard, with an automated evaluator giving real-time feedback after each submission.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Compete –&lt;/strong&gt;&amp;nbsp;Top finalists take the stage in a live, game-show-style grand finale.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Although AWS AI League offers multiple use cases, at Cisco it offered the following two challenges:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Agentic AI challenge –&lt;/strong&gt;&amp;nbsp;Participants build agents with &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/agentcore/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock AgentCore&lt;/a&gt;, a platform to build, connect, and optimize agents at scale with any framework or model. The agents move through a maze-like grid, solving challenges on the way to a treasure chest. The challenges map to real-world tasks such as handling inappropriate content, running code, retrieving information from the web, and extracting structured data. Builders can choose models for supervisor and sub-agents and add tools such as &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock-agentcore/latest/devguide/memory.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AgentCore memory, a capability of Amazon Bedrock AgentCore&lt;/a&gt;, and &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/lambda/lambda-functions/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Lambda Event Functions&lt;/a&gt;. Builders use &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/guardrails/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock Guardrails&lt;/a&gt; to apply safety controls to their agents.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Model customization challenge –&lt;/strong&gt;&amp;nbsp;Participants fine-tune models in &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/sagemaker/ai/studio/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon SageMaker Studio&lt;/a&gt; using the latest training recipes. The challenge awards points when an automated evaluator rates a participant’s customized model as more accurate and comprehensive than a reference model on the specific tasks in the competition.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Finalists are scored on time efficiency, accuracy, agent planning, and token consumption efficiency.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/03/ML-22161-1.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/03/ML-22161-1.png" alt="AWS AI League champion on stage at AWS re:Invent 2025" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 1: AWS AI League champion at AWS re:Invent 2025&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/03/ML-22161-2.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/03/ML-22161-2.png" alt="2026 AWS AI League winner at the Cisco campus" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 2: 2026 AWS AI League winner at Cisco campus&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;h1&gt;The Cisco and AWS partnership&lt;/h1&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The AWS AI League story at Cisco grows out of a deep engineering partnership. Cisco and AWS bring together networking, cloud, security, and observability for shared customers including:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Cisco SD-WAN&lt;/strong&gt;, whose controller architecture runs on AWS and serves customers globally as a software as a service (SaaS) application.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Cisco Meraki&lt;/strong&gt;, &lt;strong&gt;Cisco Spaces&lt;/strong&gt;, and Cisco’s IoT and smart buildings group.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Cisco Cloud Control&lt;/strong&gt;, a cloud-native, AI-powered application integrated with AWS services that brings together security, networking, observability, and collaboration.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Cisco AI Defense&lt;/strong&gt;, where Cisco’s security team uses &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock&lt;/a&gt; and &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/sagemaker/ai/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon SageMaker AI&lt;/a&gt; to embed AI governance and guardrails across the Cisco portfolio.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The two companies have also published joint deployment guides and Cisco Validated Designs across networking and AWS. A recent example of co-innovation is SD-WAN fabric on demand across the AWS backbone, built to deliver the resilience and uptime customers expect.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;blockquote&gt;
 &lt;p&gt;&lt;em&gt;“When you see that many business units independently choosing to build their next-generation platforms natively on AWS, that’s not a vendor relationship anymore. It’s a true engineering partnership.”&lt;/em&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
 &lt;p&gt;— Kumaril Kapoor, VP of Engineering, Cisco&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/blockquote&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;iframe loading="lazy" title="Cisco x AWS: From Transformation Fest to AI League World Champion | AWS Events" width="500" height="281" src="https://www.youtube-nocookie.com/embed/Hsdy43aYBXE?feature=oembed" frameborder="0" allow="accelerometer; autoplay; clipboard-write; encrypted-media; gyroscope; picture-in-picture; web-share" referrerpolicy="strict-origin-when-cross-origin" allowfullscreen sandbox="allow-scripts allow-same-origin"&gt;&lt;/iframe&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Cisco’s VP of Engineering Kumaril Kapoor and AWS Industries India leader Chandra Balani sit down to discuss the AWS and Cisco partnership story, from co-innovation across networking, cloud, and AI to enabling thousands of engineers through the annual Cisco AWS Transformation Fest.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h1&gt;Cisco Transformation Fest: Enablement at scale&lt;/h1&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;As the partnership grew, it extended beyond technology to people. Working closely with Cisco teams in India, AWS saw a strong appetite for learning and experimenting among Cisco engineers across many business units. The question was how to scale that impact beyond the day-to-day touchpoints of the partnership.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The answer was Cisco Transformation Fest, first held in 2022. The program is built on five tenets the team summarized as &lt;strong&gt;SCALE&lt;/strong&gt;:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;table style="height: 280px" border="1px" width="1008" cellpadding="10px"&gt; 
 &lt;tbody&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Tenet&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;What it means for Cisco engineers&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Share&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Share what you know with peers across teams.&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Connect&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Connect with engineers across business units.&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Adopt&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Adopt new skills and AWS services.&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Learn&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Learn by building, not by watching.&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Experiment / Engage&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Experiment hands-on and stay engaged with the technology and each other.&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
 &lt;/tbody&gt; 
&lt;/table&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/03/ML-22161-3-1.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/03/ML-22161-3-1.png" alt="Cisco AWS Transformation Fest 4.0 AWS AI League finalists" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 3: 2026 Cisco AWS Transformation Fest 4.0 AWS AI League finalists&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Transformation Fest brings together several formats: AWS GameDays, AWS AI League, sessions where Cisco leaders share best practices and success stories with peers, and sessions on Amazon and AWS culture. What began as a single event is now an annual, three-day program that has completed its fourth edition and reached around 2,000 engineers. AWS continues to engage Cisco teams with hands-on workshops and experience-based accelerations.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;blockquote&gt;
 &lt;p&gt;&lt;em&gt;“It has been genuinely valuable for us, and it’s really a journey, but I appreciate most is the intent behind it. It’s built around our people. It gives our engineers the time, the space, and the hands-on support to grow real skills on AWS. For us, it has turned into a lasting culture of learning by doing. It’s not a one-off event.”&lt;/em&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
 &lt;p&gt;— Kumaril Kapoor, VP of Engineering, Cisco&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/blockquote&gt; 
&lt;h1&gt;Where enablement becomes execution: AWS AI League at Cisco&lt;/h1&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Of all the Transformation Fest formats, AWS AI League has had the most visible impact. Its gamified, hands-on structure puts engineers to work with Amazon SageMaker on fine-tuning, model customization and Amazon Bedrock AgentCore for agent building, against a clock and in front of their peers. Participants leave with practical experience and genuine confidence in their AI skills.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;blockquote&gt;
 &lt;p&gt;&lt;em&gt;“This is where enablement actually turns into real execution.”&lt;/em&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
 &lt;p&gt;— Kumaril Kapoor, VP of Engineering, Cisco&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/blockquote&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="from-the-cisco-campus-to-global-champion"&gt;2025: From the Cisco campus to global champion&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Hemanth V, a software engineer in Cisco’s switching core platform team, had an inspiring journey that set the benchmark for Cisco.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;His journey had two stages:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ol type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;AWS AI League at Cisco AWS Transformation Fest 3.0.&lt;/strong&gt; He competed against more than 600 participants from Cisco in a model customization challenge, fine-tuning a small model in Amazon SageMaker, and won the title.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Global grand finale at AWS re:Invent 2025.&lt;/strong&gt; In Las Vegas he faced a completely different challenge: the agentic AI track, where he built an agent to navigate a dynamic, game-style maze. He took first place and was recognized during the re:Invent keynote.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Hemanth finished ahead of Ross Williams from Aqfer (second place) and Deepesh Khanna from Capital One (third place).&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;blockquote&gt;
 &lt;p&gt;&lt;em&gt;“You don’t need to know everything beforehand. You will learn a lot of things on the way. The only thing that matters is your curiosity and willingness to learn.”&lt;/em&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
 &lt;p&gt;— Hemanth V, Software Engineer, Cisco, and 2025 AWS AI League Champion&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/blockquote&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="engineers-one-champion"&gt;2026: 1,200 engineers, one champion&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;In 2026, Cisco Transformation Fest 4.0 hosted a full AWS AI League agentic AI tournament. The AWS account team ran pre-event training so that engineers with no prior agentic AI experience could compete from day one. The format was designed to narrow a large field down to a single winner:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;table border="1px" width="100%" cellpadding="10px"&gt; 
 &lt;tbody&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Stage&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Format&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Winning result&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Qualifier&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;1,200 participants across four cohorts. Top 5 per cohort advance to semifinal&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;1st in cohort 3 with 15,906 points (98 submissions)&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Semifinal&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;20 participants compete for 3 finalist spots&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;1st with 16,321 points&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Finale&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;3 live rounds on stage in front of Cisco and AWS leadership&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;1st with 27,008 cumulative points&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
 &lt;/tbody&gt; 
&lt;/table&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Nishanth Aluhonnu, a software engineer at Cisco, finished first at every stage and earned a full conference pass to AWS re:Invent 2026.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The finale itself showed why the format works. Participants could not change code, tools, or prompts during the finale, only a short steering instruction before each round. Success depended on how well each agent made decisions on maps it had never seen before. Nishanth lost the first round, then won the second by a wide margin by ignoring a board full of low-value coins in favor of a single high-value challenge.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/03/ML-22161-4.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/03/ML-22161-4.png" alt="Cisco AWS Transformation Fest 4.0 AWS AI League finalists on stage" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 4: Cisco AWS Transformation Fest 4.0 AWS AI League finalists on stage&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;h1&gt;Examining what it takes to win an agentic AI challenge&lt;/h1&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The 2026 winning agent offers lessons that go well beyond the game. Each design choice maps to a decision teams face when they run agents in production.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="output-tokens-are-the-real-cost"&gt;Output tokens are the real cost&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The agentic AI challenge scores agents on challenge points, a treasure bonus, remaining lives, and a token bonus, computed as &lt;code&gt;tokenBonus = 1000 - (totalOutputTokens / challengesVisited)&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;In the qualifier, the top four agents all solved the map. The first-place agent emitted 1,220 output tokens and the second-place agent emitted 1,430, and the final margin was only 16 points out of nearly 16,000. After every agent can solve the problem, efficiency decides the winner.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;To understand which tokens mattered, Nishanth built nine variants of the same agent, changing one thing at a time. The result was clear: tool results and the system prompt didn’t count against the score, but everything the agent wrote, including tool call arguments, did. Each tool call carried roughly 52 tokens of fixed overhead on top of its arguments.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;This mirrors real-world cost. Generating output is sequential and is typically priced several times higher than input, and in an agent loop that cost compounds across every step. A long, well-structured system prompt (which you can also cache) is cheap. A chatty agent isn’t.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="one-agent-not-five"&gt;One agent, not five&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;A common architecture for this challenge is a supervisor agent on Amazon Bedrock AgentCore that delegates to specialist sub-agents for navigation, computation, and web retrieval. It’s a sound pattern for many production systems. The winning design used a single agent with no sub-agents. Instead, the prompt began with a short dispatch table that matched each challenge type to the right approach in a single turn.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The reasoning was simple: every handoff between agents is a message the model has to write, and in this challenge every written token is scored. The lesson isn’t that sub-agents are bad. It’s to choose your architecture based on what coordination costs you. When coordination is expensive, a flatter design can win.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The following diagram shows the AWS reference architecture for the agentic AI challenge: a supervisor agent with specialist sub-agents. The winning design deliberately didn’t use this multi-agent pattern.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/03/ML-22161-5.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/03/ML-22161-5.png" alt="AWS AI League reference architecture with a supervisor agent and three sub-agents" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 5: AWS AI League reference architecture&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;In this reference architecture, a supervisor agent runs in Amazon Bedrock AgentCore runtime, a capability of Amazon Bedrock AgentCore, and coordinates three sub-agents, with Amazon Bedrock Guardrails and AgentCore memory connected to the runtime. The supervisor and sub-agents route through the Amazon Bedrock AgentCore Gateway, a capability of Amazon Bedrock AgentCore, to four tools: a navigation tool, browser tool, code execution tool, and custom tools using AWS Lambda functions.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="put-deterministic-logic-in-tools"&gt;Put deterministic logic in tools&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Route planning was not left to the model. It ran in an AWS Lambda function that treated the board as an optimization problem. Each tile carried an expected value: the points it paid, minus the lives it could cost, minus the tokens the agent would spend to solve it. The planner respected constraints, such as collecting a key before its door, and refined the route within a time budget.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;This is a useful pattern for most agents: let the model handle language and judgment, and move search, math, and rules into tools where they are faster, cheaper, and repeatable.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="make-decisions-on-value-not-habit"&gt;Make decisions on value, not habit&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The second finale round was designed as a trap. A seven-by-seven board was almost entirely covered in coins worth 25 points each, while a single scheduling challenge tile was worth 1,500 points. Habit said to collect the coins. The planner’s value table said the coins were noise. The agent went for the high-value challenge and scored 7,537 points in that round, compared with 5,953 for the next finalist. That round built the lead that carried the agent to the overall win.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h1&gt;Impact for Cisco&lt;/h1&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Across two years of AWS AI League at Cisco, the impact shows up in skills, culture, and recognition:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Hands-on AI skills at scale.&lt;/strong&gt; 1,200 engineers took part in the 2026 tournament, building agents on Amazon Bedrock AgentCore, many of them with no prior agentic AI experience.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Connections across business units.&lt;/strong&gt; Engineers who would otherwise never have met competed in the same tournament, shared approaches, and learned from each other.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;A lasting culture of learning.&lt;/strong&gt; Now in its fourth year, Transformation Fest has become an annual commitment rather than a one-off hackathon.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Recognition, two years in a row.&lt;/strong&gt; A Cisco engineer won the 2025 AWS AI League global title, and the 2026 Cisco champion will attend AWS re:Invent 2026.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Leadership visibility.&lt;/strong&gt; The 2026 finale took place in front of Cisco and AWS senior leadership, giving engineers a real stage for their work.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;For Hemanth, the biggest takeaway was confidence. You do not need to master everything before you start. You learn by getting hands-on.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;blockquote&gt;
 &lt;p&gt;&lt;em&gt;“Technology alone isn’t enough. You need people who are empowered to experiment with it. Transformation Fest created that enablement environment, and AWS AI League created the opportunity for execution.”&lt;/em&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
 &lt;p&gt;— Kumaril Kapoor, VP of Engineering, Cisco&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/blockquote&gt; 
&lt;h1&gt;Conclusion&lt;/h1&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;In this post, we showed how Cisco and AWS turned enablement into execution. Cisco Transformation Fest created a space for engineers to learn by doing, and AWS AI League gave them a real challenge, a scoreboard, and a stage. The result is a culture of hands-on AI building that reaches thousands of engineers.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;To get started:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Learn how to host a tournament in your organization on the &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/ai/aileague" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS AI League&lt;/a&gt; page.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Build your agentic AI and model customization skills with the AWS AI training catalog on &lt;a href="https://skillbuilder.aws/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Skill Builder&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Follow the AWS AI League 2026 global finale at AWS re:Invent 2026 in Las Vegas.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;We look forward to seeing what your teams build.&lt;/p&gt;

					<description><![CDATA[<p>Our new integrations let AI agents reason across Oracle’s business-critical data.</p>
<p>The post <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/microsoft-and-oracle-bring-agentic-ai-into-flow-of-enterprise-work/">Microsoft and Oracle bring agentic AI into flow of enterprise work</a> appeared first on <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog">Microsoft Azure Blog</a>.</p>
]]></description>
										<content:encoded><![CDATA[
<p class="wp-block-paragraph"><em>This blog is co-authored by Arpan Shah, SVP of Oracle Databases and Muneer Mirza, VP of Multicloud at Oracle Cloud Infrastructure (OCI).</em></p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Today at Microsoft Ignite, we’re bringing agentic AI into the flow of enterprise work. Our new integrations let AI agents reason across Oracle’s business-critical data—from finance and supply chain to HR and customer operations—and the work context Microsoft Work IQ understands across meetings, email, documents, and Teams. Customers can access this combined intelligence through Excel, Teams, and Microsoft Copilot. Built on <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/solutions/oracle/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Oracle AI Database@Azure</a>, the integrations help businesses turn trusted enterprise data into insights and actions, with the choice, security, and performance mission-critical workloads demand.</p>



<div class="wp-block-buttons is-content-justification-center is-layout-flex wp-container-core-buttons-is-layout-a89b3969 wp-block-buttons-is-layout-flex">
<div class="wp-block-button"><a class="wp-block-button__link wp-element-button" href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/solutions/oracle/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Learn more about Oracle on Azure</a></div>
</div>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">With Oracle AI Database@Azure, Oracle and Microsoft have helped customers modernize critical workloads to improve application performance and unlock more value from their enterprise data, by bringing databases closer to applications and connecting enterprise data with Microsoft AI. Oracle AI Database@Azure now spans 33 Azure regions, more than on any other hyperscaler, delivering the performance and flexibility customers need.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">At Oracle AI World 2026, we announced new capabilities for Oracle AI Database@Azure including FedRAMP Class D certification coming soon for US Government and public-sector workloads, and Proximity Placement Groups (PPGs) that enable customers to place their database closer to applications—resulting in approximately 50% lower VM-to-Exadata network round-trip latency than deployments without PPG alignment. Now, at Microsoft Ignite 2026, we’re building on that momentum.</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="for-it-operations-monitoring-and-trust-at-every-layer">For IT operations: Monitoring and trust at every layer</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Today, we’re announcing limited preview of <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/oracle/overview" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Azure Workload Center for Oracle</a>, bringing each workload’s architecture, resources, and dependencies together through a construct called a Workload Virtual Instance (WVI). This significantly reduces the complexity and resources required to manage solutions at the individual component level. Integrations across the Workload Virtual Instance, Azure Monitor Health Models, and the <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft Azure</a> Copilot Run Oracle agent connect monitoring signals across application, database, and infrastructure layers.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">This operational visibility is complemented by agent access controls: Oracle AI Database@Azure supports Microsoft Entra authentication and Oracle Deep Data Security, helping agents stay within users’ existing permissions while database calls run through the managed model context protocol (MCP) server and activity remains auditable across your Oracle database estate.</p>


<figure data-wp-context="{&quot;imageId&quot;:&quot;6b04ff25f38a7&quot;}" data-wp-interactive="core/image" data-wp-key="6b04ff25f38a7" class="wp-block-image aligncenter size-full wp-lightbox-container"><img decoding="async" data-wp-class--hide="state.isContentHidden" data-wp-class--show="state.isContentVisible" data-wp-init="callbacks.setButtonStyles" data-wp-on--click="actions.showLightbox" data-wp-on--load="callbacks.setButtonStyles" data-wp-on-window--resize="callbacks.setButtonStyles" src="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/azure_workload_health_upscaled.webp" alt="Azure Workload Center for Oracle showing degraded workload health for the Caldova Oracle workload. Copilot summarizes the issue and identifies database services as the cause, while the Run Oracle agent provides workload-aware troubleshooting and resolution options." class="wp-image-55525 webp-format" srcset="" data-orig-src="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/azure_workload_health_upscaled.webp"><button
			class="lightbox-trigger"
			type="button"

			aria-label="Enlarge"
			data-wp-init="callbacks.initTriggerButton"
